# CLAUDE.md

Guidance for Claude Code working in this repository.

## Tech Stack

- **Frontend**: React 18 + TypeScript + Vite + Wouter + TanStack Query + Tailwind + shadcn/ui
- **Backend**: Node.js + Express + TypeScript (tsx)
- **Database**: Self-hosted Supabase Postgres (`209.145.59.219:5433`) + Drizzle ORM
- **Auth**: Supabase GoTrue (JWT) with session fallback
- **Edge Functions**: Supabase (`supabase/functions/`)

## Directory Structure

```
client/src/{components,pages,hooks,lib}     # Frontend
server/{routes-*.ts,middleware,services}    # Backend (109 route files)
shared/{schema.ts,*-schema.ts}              # Drizzle schemas (43 files)
supabase/functions/                         # Edge Functions
drizzle/migrations/                         # Versioned SQL migrations
tests/                                      # Playwright E2E
```

**Path aliases**: `@/*` → `client/src/*` · `@shared/*` → `shared/*` · `@assets/*` → `attached_assets/*`

## Commands

```bash
# Dev / Build
npm run dev              # Backend (tsx) + frontend (Vite HMR)
npm run build            # Frontend production build
npm run build:fullstack  # Frontend + esbuild server bundle
npm run check            # tsc --noEmit
npm run lint             # ESLint
npm run format:write     # Apply Prettier

# Test
npm run test             # Vitest unit
npm run test:integration # server/tests/integration/
npm run test:e2e         # Playwright (all browsers)
npm run test:all         # unit + e2e

# Database
npm run db:generate      # Generate migration SQL
npm run db:migrate       # Apply pending migrations (locked)
npm run db:migrate:status
npm run db:push          # Direct push — DEV ONLY, never production
npm run db:backup        # Backup both DBs + retention
npm run db:restore       # Interactive restore

# Seed
npm run seed:rbac | seed:reports | seed:kpis | seed:plans
npm run stripe:setup[:live]
```

## Critical Patterns

### Multi-Tenant Isolation (SECURITY CRITICAL)

- 4-tier hierarchy: Platform → Company → Regional → Location
- 8-level roles: Platform Admin (8) → Guest (1)
- Tenant resolution: `x-tenant-id` header → JWT `app_metadata.tenantId` → Session
- **Every query MUST filter by `tenantId`** — missing filters are security vulnerabilities.

```typescript
// CORRECT
await db.query.customers.findMany({ where: eq(customers.tenantId, tenantId) });

// WRONG — leaks across tenants
await db.query.customers.findMany();
```

### Authentication Middleware

**Never define `requireAuth` locally.** Import from centralized locations.

| Middleware            | Import From                             | Use Case                                  |
| --------------------- | --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
| `requireAuth`         | `./replitAuth` or `./auth-setup`        | Standard (JWT + session fallback)         |
| `requireSupabaseAuth` | `./middleware/supabase-auth`            | Strict JWT only                           |
| `protectedRoute`      | `./middleware/supabase-auth`            | JWT + Auth + Tenant context (recommended) |
| `platformAdminRoute`  | `./middleware/supabase-auth`            | Platform admin only                       |
| `requirePermission`   | `./middleware/enhanced-rbac-middleware` | RBAC checks                               |

```typescript
import { requireAuth } from '../replitAuth';
import { getUserId, getTenantId } from '../utils/auth-helpers';

app.get('/api/resource', requireAuth, async (req, res) => {
  const tenantId = getTenantId(req);
  const data = await db.query.table.findMany({ where: eq(table.tenantId, tenantId) });
});
```

**Auth helpers** (`utils/auth-helpers`): `getUserId`, `getTenantId`, `isAuthenticated`, `isPlatformAdmin`.

### RBAC Permissions

Format: `<module>.<resource>.<action>_<scope>` — e.g. `sales.lead.view_own`, `sales.quote.approve_standard`.

```typescript
app.get('/leads', requirePermission(['sales.lead.view_own', 'sales.lead.view_team']), handler);
```

### Unified Business Records

Leads and customers share `business_records`. Status field determines state. Lead-to-customer conversion = status update (preserves history).

TWO TABLES HOLDING ONE CONCEPT IS A COUNTING PROBLEM BEFORE IT IS A MIGRATION (COP-B00). `business_records` was declared canonical and nothing carried it out, and the story sat filed as a dev/prod disagreement until somebody counted: **53 edge functions read `business_records`; 7 touch `companies`, and three of those WRITE it** (`business-records`, `companies`, `import`). `business-records` is the function behind the production CRM account list and its own header calls itself "a backwards-compatible wrapper that delegates to the companies table", so an account created there lands where the opportunity radar, churn risk, QBR, contracts, invoices, service, search and the forecast never look. Count the readers and the writers before believing a story's framing - `grep -rln "\.from('<table>')" supabase/functions/ | sed 's|.*functions/||;s|/.*||' | sort -u` took seconds and changed what the story was. `npm run crm:reconcile` (COP-B00) is the tooling, and its design is the reusable part: it SEPARATES "does this row already exist over there" from "what would it look like there", and answers the first with EXACT keys only. An id match is proof; a `customer_number` match is proof because the column is UNIQUE on both; a NAME match is a CANDIDATE that is reported and SKIPPED, because COP-B10 and COP-B09 both record what it costs to let a migration decide unreviewed that two spellings are one thing - here it fuses two customer accounts. THE ID IS PRESERVED: `deals`, `proposals` and `quotes` all carry an account id, so copying under a fresh uuid orphans every reference the moment the readers switch, and keeping it makes the copy idempotent for nothing. NOTHING IS INVENTED TO SATISFY A NOT NULL - a row with no `created_by` is refused and counted rather than given a sentinel owner, and `source` is written as 'migrated' because the column's 'website' default would claim the account arrived through the web form. A free-text column mapped into a vocabulary (`companies.activity` -> `business_records.status`) COERCES BY RECORD TYPE and COUNTS the coercions in the report; the lead and customer vocabularies do not overlap, so 'qualified' on a customer row is a lead status on the wrong row. Runbook and what still needs the deployed database: `docs/COP-B00-crm-table-reconciliation.md`.

**Canonical CRM object model:** the CRM is mid-migration with duplicate models per entity. The canonical table for each entity (contact = `companyContacts`, company/account = `business_records`, pipeline = `deals` + `pipelineStages`/`pipelineTemplates`) and the deprecated duplicates + migration path are recorded in `docs/crm-canonical-model.md` (CRMX-002). Deprecated tables carry `@deprecated` banners. Bind new CRM work to the canonical tables only.

**Workflow automation runtime (CRMX-008):** the real execution engine lives in `server/services/workflow-execution-service.ts` (durable step executor) + `server/services/workflow-runtime.ts` (dispatch/enroll/sweeper), over the 15-table `shared/workflow-automation-schema.ts`. The registered router is `server/routes/workflow-automation-routes.ts` (mounted at `/api` by `routes-registry.ts`); the top-level `server/routes-workflow-automation.ts` was **unregistered dead mock** and PROD-008b deleted it, so `routes/workflow-automation-routes.ts` is now the only workflow router in the tree. REGISTERED IS NOT REACHABLE, and the distinction matters here more than anywhere (SEC-SESSION-001): all 35 of its handlers read `req.session.user` as their only identity and nothing assigns it, so every one answers 401 in dev as well as prod, and no client tree calls `/api/workflows`, `/api/executions`, `/api/triggers` or `/api/approvals` either. The RUNTIME below is genuinely live and independent of it - dispatch comes from the edge seams and the sweeper, never through this router - so workflows execute while nobody can create, inspect or approve one. TWO DISPATCHERS, ONE PER HOST, and picking the wrong one means the event never fires. **A THIRD ONE EXISTED AND QUALITY-002 DELETED IT**: `server/services/workflow-event-service.ts`, 394 lines, unreachable, describing itself as the thing that "finds matching workflow triggers and initiates execution" - so the risk was never that it ran, it was that somebody would wire it. `server/tests/unit/dead-workflow-and-rbac-retired.test.ts` now scans `server/services/` for anything else making that claim. It also could not have run: it imported the TABLE objects from `@shared/schema`, which re-exports the workflow types and insert schemas but NOT the tables, so every query resolved to `never`. **A cluster of "does not exist on type 'never'" on a Drizzle query is a signature** - it usually means the table import resolved to nothing, not that the query is wrong. Node code calls `dispatchWorkflowEvent(...)` from `server/services/workflow-runtime.ts`; EDGE code calls `dispatchWorkflowEventSafe(...)` from `supabase/functions/_shared/workflow-dispatch.ts`. Both carry the kill switch and the dedupe-key idempotency verbatim, and their shared condition evaluator is locked by `server/tests/unit/workflow-conditions-parity.test.ts`. Wrap either in try/catch so automation never breaks the write that triggered it. SEAMS WIRED TODAY (CRMX-008a moved them; the Express ones this line used to name are gone — `server/routes-deals.ts` was deleted outright): `deal.stage_changed` in `supabase/functions/deals/index.ts`, `record.created`/`record.updated` in `supabase/functions/business-records/index.ts`, and `form.submitted` from the Node side in `server/services/web-form-processor.ts`. Those are the ONLY production call sites — grep `dispatchWorkflowEvent` and everything else you find is a test. STILL OPEN (CRMX-008a): an edge enrolment can only QUEUE. The step executor (`workflow-execution-service.ts`) is Node-only and `supabase/functions/workflows`' `/execute` returns 501 on purpose, so what advances an edge-enrolled execution is an unresolved deployment decision, not an oversight. Executions are durable (DB-persisted, `wait_delay`/approval PAUSE with `resume_at`/`context._runtime` cursor, resumed by the boot-started sweeper — no in-process `setTimeout`) and idempotent (atomic `queued|paused→running` claim + `workflow_executions.dedupe_key` unique index, migration 0025). Kill switch = `workflows.status !== 'active'` (enrollment skips) + per-execution `POST /api/executions/:id/cancel`.

## Database

### Migration Workflow

1. Edit schema in `shared/`
2. `npm run db:generate` → creates SQL in `drizzle/migrations/`
3. Review the SQL
4. `npm run db:migrate` (acquires `__migration_lock`, auto-expires after 5min)
5. Commit schema + migration file together

**Journal integrity (SUPA-005):** drizzle's migrator only applies migrations listed in `drizzle/migrations/meta/_journal.json`. A `*.sql` file with no journal entry is silently NEVER applied → schema drift. `npm run check:migrations` (CI guard) fails when any migration file is unjournaled, two files share a 4-digit prefix, or journal `idx` is non-contiguous. Always add migrations via `db:generate` (which updates the journal) — never hand-drop a `.sql` into `drizzle/migrations/`.

**Snapshot integrity (COP-M00):** drizzle-kit diffs your schema against the snapshot of the LAST journal entry only. That snapshot went missing and `db:generate` silently re-emitted 445 statements of already-applied drift, numbered into a filename collision, so no schema change could ship. Fixed by capturing the drift as a real migration (`0057_cop_m00_schema_reconciliation.sql`, fully idempotent, 118 tables + 15 columns + 9 FKs) rather than absorbing it into a snapshot, which would have left 107 tables permanently un-migrated. `npm run db:generate` is now `scripts/db-generate.mjs`, which renumbers whatever drizzle-kit writes to (highest on-disk prefix + 1) and rewrites the journal tag — drizzle-kit names files from journal LENGTH, and the non-contiguous on-disk numbering is load-bearing (SUPA-002 sequenced `when` values so orphans sort after everything applied). `check:migrations` now HARD-fails when the last entry has no snapshot; the 25 mid-chain gaps stay a warned ratchet. `0057` is committed but UNAPPLIED anywhere — runbook in `docs/COP-M00-migration-tooling.md`. That doc also records the separate finding that `npm run db:migrate` cannot build a fresh database at all: it fails in migration `0000` on uuid-vs-varchar FK key columns and, being one transaction, creates nothing (PA-032).

A tenant_id COLUMN HAS TO BE ABLE TO HOLD A TENANT ID (AUDIT-032). `tenants.id` is `varchar PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()`; 21 tables across four schema files declared `tenant_id integer NOT NULL`, so a read filtered on a real tenant gave **22P02** and an insert gave **42804** - both reproduced on Postgres 16, not inferred. Nothing caught it: tsc cannot, because the column type and the `tenantId` variable agree inside each file and the Express layer types `tenantId` as `any`; `check:phantom-cols` checks column NAMES, not types; and PostgREST leaves `.data` null on error while this code writes `?? []` around it, so the symptom was an EMPTY DASHBOARD, indistinguishable from a table nobody has filled in. That is why six of them - `client_collected_metrics`, `contract_renewal_tracking`, `renewal_analytics`, `supply_monitoring`, `supply_replenishment_analytics`, `supply_usage_history` - sat in `docs/unwritten-tables-baseline.json` as tables nothing writes to, when the truth is that nothing COULD. The affected features are auto-supply replenishment, contract renewal (including the routed `/contract-renewal-autopilot`), document automation and the printyx-client agent. Migration `0062_round_sway.sql` converts all 21 and was PROVEN by applying it to a real Postgres twice, including against a database that had already run the hand-run `_inbound_webhook_events.sql` (which drizzle-kit correctly folded in, since an unjournaled file is invisible to the snapshot - it is `IF NOT EXISTS` for that reason). `npm run check:tenant-id-types` gates both sides at zero, because either alone is wrong here: it reads the Drizzle declarations AND replays the migration chain in journal order, since `0000` creates these columns as integer and `0062` converts them. The workaround the bad type forced is the thing to recognise elsewhere: `predictive-service-dispatch-service.ts` did `eq(clientCollectedMetrics.tenantId, parseInt(tenantId))` to satisfy tsc, and `parseInt` of a uuid is its leading digits (`'550e8400-...'` -> 550) or NaN, so it filtered on a tenant that does not exist. When a tenant id needs coercing, the column is wrong, not the id.

**A scratch Postgres is available, and several "blocked on DB access" stories are not (COP-M07):** this container ships PostgreSQL 16 at `/usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin`. `initdb` refuses to run as root, so run it as the `postgres` user (`su postgres -c "... initdb -D <dir> -U postgres --auth=trust"`), start it on a spare port, apply the migrations statement-tolerantly (`psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=0 -f` in journal order — `db:migrate` itself dies in `0000`, see above), then insert one `tenants` + `users` row and run `DEMO_USER=<email> npm run seed:demo`. That is enough to verify anything that needs real rows. Two traps it exposes: the demo seeder short-circuits with "Demo data already exists" for a tenant that has any, and `generateId()` produces TENANT-INDEPENDENT ids (`stage-demo-001`) under `onConflictDoNothing`, so seeding a SECOND tenant into the same database reports success and writes NOTHING. Use a fresh database per tenant you want to seed.

**Pipeline stages (COP-M07):** `deals.stage_id` holds a LEGACY `deal_stages.id`; `pipeline_stages.legacy_stage_id` is the bridge CRMX-005 established. `npm run check:stage-resolution` (needs `DATABASE_URL`) asserts every deal resolves to exactly one canonical stage and exits **2** when it cannot connect, so "did not run" is never read as "passed" — run it before and after touching any stage writer. Three defects this closed, each reproduced against a real database: `pipeline_templates.pipeline_type` is NOT NULL and the lazy bootstrap in `supabase/functions/pipeline-config/index.ts` omitted it, so that bootstrap could NEVER succeed and any tenant without a template had a permanently empty board; the explicit template create wrote `stage_type` and `allowed_transitions`, neither of which is a column on `pipeline_stages`, so every create returned "Template saved but stage insert failed" and orphaned the template; and the read paths returned raw rows, so the Stage Type select always showed its placeholder. `stageType` now maps to `is_final_stage`/`is_closed_won`/`is_closed_lost`/`is_active` through `supabase/functions/_shared/pipeline-stage-type.ts`. `allowed_transitions` was dropped rather than given a column — nothing reads it.

`db:push` is dev-only. For an existing DB without migrations: generate baseline, then `npm run db:migrate:baseline` (marks applied without executing).

### Backups

`pg_dump` + gzip → GCS. Naming: `printyx-backup-YYYY-MM-DD-HHmmss.sql.gz`. Retention: daily 7d / weekly 4w / monthly 12m. K8s CronJob (`k8s/base/cronjob-backup.yaml`) runs daily 02:00 UTC.

Restore requires interactive confirmation (double for production). Set `RESTORE_TARGET_DB` to redirect target.

Env: `BACKUP_GCS_BUCKET` (default `printyx-backups`), `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`.

THOSE SCRIPTS HAD NEVER BEEN RUN, AND THREE THINGS WERE WRONG (LAUNCH-011, round 108). Four of that story's criteria said "NEEDS OPS TESTING" and the block above described behaviour nobody had exercised. **PostgreSQL 16 is in this container**, so the local half is runnable (the COP-M07 recipe): real migrator, 683 tables, seeded rows, then backup, list, restore into a separate database, retention with aged files. **(1) The forecasting backup was a copy of the main database.** `run_backup "$DB_NAME" "printyx-forecast-backup"` carried no `--schema`, so `npm run db:backup` wrote two 228K archives with the same 683 tables, one NAMED as the forecast, and logged success - while `k8s/base/cronjob-backup.yaml` has always passed `--schema=forecasting`. The hand-run script and the nightly job wrote **different artifacts under the same name into the same GCS folder**, so restoring "the latest forecast backup" depends on which ran last. **(2) Local retention deleted weekly and monthly archives at seven days** - a flat `find -mtime +7 -delete` under a policy both the script and `db-backup-list.sh` print as 7d/4w/12m, so a GCS-less deployment had neither tier. The tier now comes from the date in the FILENAME (what the GCS branch already keys on), so one archive serves all three; an archive with no parseable date is KEPT. **(3) The CronJob's emptiness guard could not fail**: `|| echo` swallows pg_dump's status even under `set -o pipefail`, and **gzip of empty input is 20 bytes**, which `[ -s ]` calls non-empty - so a dump that never ran uploaded a 20-byte archive and logged "uploaded". Use a byte floor, never `-s`, on anything that came out of a pipe.

AND ONE CLAIM WAS OVERSTATED AND CAUGHT BEFORE IT SHIPPED, which is the part worth copying. The first draft added `set -o pipefail` inside `db-backup.sh`'s dump with a comment saying failure detection depended on it. **It did not** - line 23 already sets it globally, and pointing the script at a database that does not exist gives exit 1, "backup FAILED" and no file left behind. The redundant flag was REMOVED rather than kept just in case, because keeping it implies the global one is not enough. Three of four claims survived being proved against the thing itself and the fourth did not - which is the whole argument for proving them, and this file already says so about predictions made in a shell.

## API Conventions

### Versioning

URL prefix: `/api/v1/leads`. Header alternative: `X-API-Version: v1` or `Accept-Version: v1`. Resolution: URL > X-API-Version > Accept-Version > default v1. All responses set `X-API-Version`. Deprecated versions add `Deprecation` and `Sunset` headers. `GET /api/versions` lists supported versions.

### REST

```
GET    /api/[resource]      # List (paginated)
GET    /api/[resource]/:id
POST   /api/[resource]
PUT    /api/[resource]/:id  # Full update
PATCH  /api/[resource]/:id  # Partial
DELETE /api/[resource]/:id
```

### Error Format

```json
{ "message": "...", "code": "ERROR_CODE", "details": {}, "requestId": "uuid" }
```

### New Route File

1. Create `server/routes-[feature].ts`, register in `server/routes.ts`
2. Apply `requireAuth` (or stronger), `requireTenant`, `requirePermission`
3. Validate input with Zod

## Frontend

- shadcn/ui from `client/src/components/ui/`
- Mobile-first: sm(640) md(768) lg(1024) xl(1280); touch targets ≥48px; mobile components in `client/src/components/mobile/`
- Server state: TanStack Query · Forms: React Hook Form + Zod · Local: useState/useReducer · Real-time: `useWebSocket`
- Key hooks: `useAuth`, `usePaginatedQuery`, `useOptimisticMutations`, `useWebSocket`

## Supabase

- API: `https://api.printyx.net` · Edge Functions: `https://functions.printyx.net` · DB pooler: `209.145.59.219:5433`
- Required env: `DATABASE_URL`, `DB_SSL=true`, `DB_SSL_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=false`, `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`

## Feature Modules

### Address Book Manager (ABK-###)

Multi-vendor MFP address book import/edit/export. Source PRD: `tasks/prd-address-book-manager.md`.

- Schema: `shared/address-book-schema.ts` · Types: `shared/address-book-types.ts`
- Edge function: `supabase/functions/address-books/index.ts`
- Credential vault (AES-256-GCM): `server/services/address-book/credential-vault.ts` (env: `ADDRESS_BOOK_MASTER_KEY`)

### Platform Admin Blog System (US-BLOG-###)

Fully autonomous content marketing platform for Printyx's marketing site. Source PRD: `blog-system-prd.json`. Merge tool: `scripts/merge-blog-prd.mjs`.

**Layout:**

- `shared/blog-schema.ts` — 15 Drizzle tables; re-exported from `shared/schema.ts`
- `supabase/functions/_shared/blog/` — adapters + helpers shared across edge functions
  - `audit-log.ts` — `writeAuditLog(admin, withRequestContext(req, entry))` — every mutating blog endpoint MUST use this
  - `safety/kill-switch.ts` — `assertAgentsActive(admin, tenantId, agentKind)` — every agent entry point MUST call this before any work
  - `cms/` — `CmsAdapter` contract + WordPress + Ghost adapters + registry
  - `keyword/` — `KeywordAdapter` contract + DataForSEO adapter + registry
- `supabase/functions/_shared/credential-vault.ts` — Deno Web Crypto AES-256-GCM. Env: `PRINTYX_CREDENTIAL_VAULT_KEY` (or legacy `ADDRESS_BOOK_MASTER_KEY`). Used by every edge function that persists a third-party credential.
- `supabase/functions/blog-*/` — one directory per edge function: `blog-agents` (kill switch), `blog-brand-voices`, `blog-style-guides`, `blog-cms-targets`, `blog-keyword-targets`, `blog-audit-log`, `blog-onboarding`
- `client/src/pages/platform-admin/blog/` — admin UI pages
- `client/src/components/blog/` — `BlogShell` (sub-nav), `BlogSettingsNav` (settings sub-nav), `BlogOnboardingChecklist`, `BlogSectionPlaceholder`
- `client/src/lib/blog/` — frontend helpers (`brand-voice-prompt.ts`)

**Routes:** `/platform-admin/blog` — gated to platform admin via `ProtectedRoute platformOnly` and sidebar `platformOnly: true`. Sub-routes: `/ideas`, `/briefs`, `/posts`, `/distribution`, `/analytics`, `/refresh`, `/settings/*`. Settings hub has 6 sub-pages: agents, brand-voices, style-guides, cms-targets, keyword-targets, audit-log.

**v1 adapters:** WordPress + Ghost (CMS), DataForSEO (keyword), X/Twitter + LinkedIn (social, not yet wired). Phase 2: Webflow, Ahrefs, SEMrush, Meta, TikTok, Pinterest, Reddit, HN/Medium/Substack.

**Autonomy:** Full closed loop with auto-refresh agent (US-BLOG-066) gated behind kill switch (US-BLOG-086). Per scope decision 5D: auto-refresh defaults to fully autonomous (no human review); the per-tenant `auto_refresh_requires_review` flag flips to require review.

**Permissions:** `blog.post.{view,edit,publish,delete}`, `blog.{brand_voice,style_guide}.edit`, `blog.distribution.publish`, `blog.analytics.view`, `blog.refresh.manage`, `blog.agent.toggle`. Granted by default to PLATFORM_ADMIN (all 10) and COMPANY_ADMIN (editorial subset; no destructive or kill-switch perms).

**Patterns established for new blog edge functions:**

1. Auth: `createSupabaseClient(req)` → `auth.getUser(jwt)`. Permission gate via the role-string + `app_metadata.permissions[]` hybrid (e.g., `'blog.brand_voice.edit'`).
2. Tenant: read from `app_metadata.tenantId` with fallbacks; never trust query params.
3. DB writes: use `createSupabaseServiceClient()` (bypasses RLS). Filter every query by `tenant_id`.
4. Audit: every mutating action calls `writeAuditLog(admin, withRequestContext(req, entry))`. Don't insert directly into `blog_audit_log`.
5. Soft delete: set `deleted_at`; filter reads with `.is('deleted_at', null)`.
6. Default-row uniqueness: atomically clear other defaults in the same edge function call.
7. Encrypted creds: `encryptCredential(plaintext)` → `encrypted_config` jsonb on the row. Never return decrypted creds; return `*_set: boolean` markers instead.
8. Audit log is append-only at the DB layer (`drizzle/rls/blog.sql` drops UPDATE/DELETE policies on `blog_audit_log`).
9. File uploads go THROUGH the function, never past it (SEC-SVG-002). `blog-assets` used to hand the client a `createSignedUploadUrl` and take `mime_type` as a declared string on a separate metadata call, so nothing validated what landed - not the type, not the size, not the content - in a bucket whose objects are read back with `getPublicUrl`, with `image/svg+xml` offered in the picker. Validating afterwards does not close that: the client can simply never call the metadata endpoint, and the object is public by then. `POST /blog-assets/upload` takes the bytes as multipart, sniffs them with `sniffUpload` from `_shared/upload-validation.ts`, caps size (25MB, 2MB for SVG), runs `sanitizeSvg`, and stores under the SNIFFED content type and extension. A DECLARED TYPE IS NOT EVIDENCE: `file.type` is set by the browser from the extension and by any other client to whatever it likes, so `branding-profiles` now sniffs too rather than keying off it. `POST /blog-assets` reads `mime_type`/`file_size_bytes` off the stored object and refuses a `storage_path` with nothing behind it. `/blog-assets/upload-url` answers 410. Multipart from the browser needs `apiFormRequest`, not `apiRequest` (which JSON-stringifies its body).

### Edge path normalization (prod-only 404s) — READ BEFORE TOUCHING ANY EDGE FN

An edge handler NEVER sees its own name in the path. The Coolify dispatcher
(`supabase/functions/server.ts`) resolves the function from URL segment 0 and then
**strips it** (`stripSegments = 1`; it rewrites to `'/' + pathParts.slice(1).join('/')`)
before invoking the handler. So `functions.printyx.net/seo/settings` arrives as
`/settings`.

Therefore **`const resource = url.pathname.split('/').filter(Boolean)[1]` is a bug.**
It reads `undefined`, every `resource === '...'` guard is false, and the function 404s
on EVERY route. Always use the idempotent helper instead — it strips an OPTIONAL
leading prefix, so routing is identical whether the segment is present or already gone:

```typescript
import { normalizePath } from '../_shared/path.ts';
const { parts } = normalizePath(url.pathname, 'seo'); // parts[0] = resource
```

**This is invisible in dev.** If the prefix is not in `crmProxies`, Express serves it
locally and the edge function is never exercised — the outage is prod-only. ALL 169 edge functions are now migrated (2026-07-17); `npm run check:edge-paths` enforces it at 0. seo and deal-desk were each 404ing on EVERY endpoint before that sweep. The impact claim is statically derived — the mechanism is proven but nothing was executed against a deploy, so confirm with one curl per prefix before treating it as an incident writeup.

GPT-5 endpoints (AI-001): `/api/ai/gpt5/*` is served by `supabase/functions/ai-gpt5/` — a `server.ts` alias (`functionName === 'ai' && subPath[0] === 'gpt5'`) plus a `crmProxies` entry `'/api/ai/gpt5': { fn: 'ai-gpt5', pathPrefix: '/gpt5' }`. Only that sub-path is proxied; the rest of `/api/ai` stays on Express and has no frontend caller. Prompt text, model configs and the response parser are duplicated in `_shared/gpt5-prompts.ts` (+ `_shared/crisis-response.ts`) and locked to `server/services/gpt5-service.ts` by `server/tests/unit/gpt5-prompts-parity.test.ts`. The edge fn needs `OPENAI_API_KEY` in the edge environment or every endpoint 500s. Two traps this closed: the Responses API `output` is an ARRAY (`response.output?.content` is always undefined — use `extractResponseText`, since the raw REST payload carries no `output_text`), and `check:routes` classifies per DOMAIN, so a multi-segment proxy prefix only counts when EVERY live frontend path under that domain sits inside it — `route-parity.mjs` now enforces that rather than whitelisting.

Phantom PostgREST columns (COP-M01): edge functions name columns in STRINGS, so a column that does not exist is a runtime 42703 — a 500 the moment the code path runs — invisible to tsc, lint and any test that does not hit a database. `npm run check:phantom-cols` (`scripts/check-phantom-columns.ts`) reads all 671 Drizzle tables and checks every column literal an edge fn hands to PostgREST (`.eq/.order/.or/.select` and INLINE `.insert/.update` object literals) against the table its call chain is on; baseline `docs/phantom-columns-baseline.json` at **384**, do not grow it. Known blind spots, stated so a pass is never read as proof of correctness: payloads built as a named variable (the deals insert was one), runtime-assembled names, camel→snake field maps, the 107 tables in no Drizzle schema, and 15 tables declared twice with different shapes (skipped as ambiguous rather than reported — that is `check:dup-tables`' story). Confirmed true positives worth knowing: `users` has `first_name`/`last_name` and `is_active`, NOT `name`/`full_name`/`status`/`phone`/`job_title`; `roles` has NO `tenant_id`; `audit_logs` has `timestamp`, not `created_at`; `deals` is queried as `deal_value`/`stage`/`value`/`name`/`closed_at` by eight more edge fns (commission, crm-goals, dashboards, pipeline, sales-reports, today-dashboard, user-assignments, pipeline-config). Failure mode to recognise: PostgREST leaves `.data` null on error and this code says `|| 0`, so the symptom is a dashboard of zeroes, not an error.

Fabricated fallback rows (EDGE-002g): the `.data`-is-null failure mode above has a worse sibling — an error branch that answers with hand-written objects at status 200, which no caller can tell from real data. `auto-lead-routing` returned two "sample" routing rules for `lead_routing_rules`, a relation named by no schema, no migration and no other file in the tree, so the routing UI listed rules that did not exist; `commission` returned a "Sales Rep Standard" plan with 5%/6.5%/8% tiers, which is a rep reading invented numbers as their own pay structure. `npm run check:phantom-tables` now hard-gates both patterns at zero: an undeclared table queried by a function that swallows 42P01/PGRST205, AND an `if (error)` block returning an array-of-objects literal. Neither instance tripped the first rule, because neither tested the error code. The predicate for the swallow itself is now one definition in `supabase/functions/_shared/postgrest-errors.ts` (`isMissingTableError`/`isMissingColumnError`), re-exported by the billing and customer-portal `_context.ts` copies. Prefer 503 over 500 when a relation is missing: the request is well formed and will work once it exists.

Nobody fills this in (AUDIT-028). Beyond a phantom table and a fabricated fallback there is a third silent-empty shape: a correct read of a real table that no code anywhere writes. `system_alerts` is the worked example — `supabase/functions/performance/` read it correctly, the only insert against it is `storage.createSystemAlert`, and no caller names that, so SystemAlertBell, PageAlerts and the notification-bell fallback were permanently empty in production while `routes-operations-extended.ts` derived four real alert families (low stock, dispatch delay, overdue invoices, contracts ending within 90 days) and served them in DEV ONLY, because `/api/performance` was not proxied. The two environments disagreed about whether the business had any problems and the one saying "none" was production. Those derivations now live in the edge function alongside the stored read, each family independently try/caught so one missing table cannot blank the bell, and low stock capped because PostgREST cannot compare two columns (`quantity_on_hand <= reorder_point` is filtered in the handler). `npm run check:unwritten-tables` reports the rest: **33 declared tables an edge function reads and nothing inserts into**, baseline `docs/unwritten-tables-baseline.json`. Each entry is a QUESTION — who fills this in? — with four possible answers: a UI never built, an importer never wired, an external feed (E-Automate) that should be named in a comment, or a derivation that should join the read. It cannot see a writer that EXISTS but is unreachable, which is what `system_alerts` was, so the true number is larger than 33. Live ones worth knowing: `contract_renewal_tracking` (the routed /contract-renewal-autopilot dashboard analyses contracts that nothing ever creates), `kpi_values` (the seeder fills `kpi_definitions`, never the values, so every KPI is permanently null — honestly null, at least), and all three of auto-supply-replenishment's inputs.

Check before you build a guard. `scripts/check-phantom-tables.mjs` and its baseline already existed, wired into CI, with a rule the replacement did not have — and a from-scratch rewrite landed on top of them before `git diff --stat` showed the files as MODIFIED rather than new. `ls scripts/ | grep <topic>` and `grep -n <topic> .github/workflows/ci.yml` cost nothing. Second trap from the same hour, and the third time this session: a guard whose corpus includes `scripts/` will read its OWN header, so an absence assertion that names its worked example in prose quietly clears that example. Strip comments on both sides of any such check.

A TABLE NAME IN A RAW SQL STRING IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY GUARD HERE (CR-017). `routes-operations-extended.ts` held NINE tables that exist in no Drizzle schema and no migration - `commission_payments`, `commission_structures`, `commission_transactions`, `sales_quotas`, `sales_representatives`, `iot_devices`, `predictive_alerts`, `equipment_status_monitoring`, `device_performance_trends` - every one inside a template literal, so all nineteen handlers were a permanent 500 in dev. tsc cannot see into a string; `check:phantom-cols` reads edge functions only; and a first scan for `.from(<identifier>)` reported ZERO phantom tables in that file. Grep for `FROM <name>` / `INSERT INTO <name>` inside string literals, not just for Drizzle identifiers. `npm run check:sql-string-tables` now closes that blind spot and is the guard to run after touching any raw-SQL handler. It extracts string LITERALS, keeps only those that parse as SQL, and resolves every `FROM`/`JOIN`/`INSERT INTO`/`UPDATE` target against `pgTable` declarations plus `CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE VIEW` across the whole migration chain. Baseline **90**, of which 78 are `database-updater/seeders/`, which STORE SQL for report definitions to run later rather than executing it; the other twelve are live reporting services naming `activities`, `quotas`, `sales_quotas`, `parts_usage` and `ai_tasks`. Three false-positive classes are handled by RULE rather than baselined, since a baseline holding known non-defects is where a real one hides: CTE names (`WITH x AS`), collected per FILE because these queries interpolate and split one statement across two template literals; aliases and set-returning functions; and `EXTRACT(field FROM column)`, whose trap is which side the name is on - the COLUMN follows `FROM`, so listing date-part words as non-relations reads it backwards and reports `payment_date` as a missing table. Without the is-this-SQL filter the first cut reported **515** references, nearly all English prose ("update the baseline", "read from the schema").

A METRIC CAN BE A TAUTOLOGY (PA-040). `platform-analytics`' revenue-metrics computed `estimatedCAC = ltv / 3` and then `ltvCacRatio = ltv / estimatedCAC`, which is **exactly 3.00 for every tenant on every request** - a constant wearing a derivation's clothes, with `paybackPeriod` built on the same figure. All three are null now and named in `unbacked`, along with NRR (`expansionRate` was hardcoded 0 because `business_records` has no `last_mrr_change`, so NRR was GRR under another name) and the growth rates (the endpoint reads one window; `growth-trends` returns the real series). LTV is null when nothing has churned, since average lifetime is 1/churn and the 24-month default was a number somebody picked. Worth checking for elsewhere: a "derived" metric whose inputs are themselves derived from it. The same page also showed 1450 calls / 2340 emails / 687 meetings typed in **while `performance-metrics` was already returning real `activityTotals` and `activityAvgPerDay`** off `platform_activity_reports` and nothing read them - so before wiring a fabricated card, check whether its endpoint already sends the number.

A FALLBACK IS ONLY A FALLBACK IF THE PRIMARY PATH CAN WIN (PA-040). `PlatformCohortAnalysis` DID call its endpoint, which is exactly what hid it: the endpoint returned `{ cohorts, summary }` and the page read `cohortTable` / `revenueCohorts` / `ltvData`, so all three resolved to undefined and the `?? [...]` arrays were not a degradation path but the ONLY path, in dev and production alike. A platform admin saw 79.4% three-month retention, $20,605 LTV, a 6.92:1 ratio, a curve from 100 down to 56.2, and a recommendation to scale acquisition spend because "CAC has decreased by 18% while LTV has increased by 23%" - every number typed in. THREE GUARDS EACH HELD A PIECE and none could see it: `check:fabricated` baselined the three `??` reads, `check:no-static-posture` the four headline cards, CR-033 had annotated the shape - none of them compares the KEY NAMES a page reads against the ones its endpoint sends. When a page has both a query and a `||` fallback, check the key names; that combination reads as defensive and can be total. **Second lesson, on what a table can answer**: `platform_cohort_analysis` holds ONE ROW PER COHORT and no per-period history, so a month0..month6 retention matrix, a revenue-by-month chart and an M0..M12 curve are all underivable - they were removed rather than wired, with the gap named in an `unbacked` array the page renders. Two of the page's three selectors were inert as well (`type=` and `metric=` were sent on every request and the endpoint reads neither); they are gone, and `timeframe` now computes the `startDate` it sends. **The harder version of the same shape**, found a day later in the same story: `analytics/ConversionInsights` and `analytics/PipelineTrendWidgets`, both rendered inside the live `LeadDeals` board, wrapped four requests in `select: (data) => data || {...}`. In production all four 404 - the `analytics` edge fn answers only `dashboard`, `sales`, `service`, `performance` - and in dev they were served by `server/analytics-routes.ts`, 184 lines and four handlers with NO DATABASE IMPORT AT ALL, returning the same objects the components already held. Both environments therefore agreed, which is why nobody looked: 65% lead-to-qualified, a 45-day cycle, four ranked loss reasons, and control charts with sigma bands. A control chart is a claim about process stability; drawn over invented points it asserts something specific and false. So before treating a `|| literal` as defensive, check that its endpoint EXISTS and reads the keys the page reads - the tell here was a router with no db import. Both components are `NotConnectedState` gates now, the router is deleted, and `server/tests/unit/analytics-fiction-removed.test.ts` locks it; `check:fabricated` 9 -> 2.

A GUARD SCOPED TO ONE DIRECTORY MISSES THE NEXT PAGE (PA-040). `check:no-static-posture` watched `client/src/pages/admin` plus four named files - the surface AUDIT-019 happened to be cleaning - while PA-040 listed nine MORE pages elsewhere rendering mock constants with no API call. Picking pages off by name is how the next one gets missed, so the scope is now the whole of `client/src/pages`: **58 values across 16 pages**, baseline **49** (shrink-only, keyed by file + text so moving code does not churn it). Components stay out of scope by design - a presentational component takes its values through props and scanning them buries the finding in noise. `client/src/pages/marketing/*` is excluded BY RULE rather than baselined: it is public copy ("52 integrations", "24/7") read as a claim about the product, not as a measurement of a tenant's data, and baselining it would mean hand-adding every future marketing page. Worth knowing what is in there: `PlatformCohortAnalysis` shows 79.4% retention and $20,605 LTV as typed literals on a platform-admin analytics screen, and `EquipmentLifecycleHub` has eight. Fixed in that pass: `BusinessProcessOptimization`'s "Quality Metrics" card - four percentages with a progress bar each, ending in **Audit Readiness 96.1%** - on a page whose endpoint is a fixture that 404s in production. Nothing measures documentation coverage, adherence, training completion or audit readiness; an audit-readiness figure with nothing behind it is LEGAL-010's case, so the card was deleted rather than relabelled. Also worth re-checking before picking up any story that names pages: seven of PA-040's nine were already deleted, wired or gated, and the list had gone stale.

CUSTOMER DETAIL SUB-RESOURCES (PA-020). `/api/customers/:id/<thing>` is served by `supabase/functions/customers/`, which now routes the sub-segment - it used to compute `customerId` and drop it, so every tab (invoices, equipment, service history, financials, supplies) fell into the single-customer branch and got the CUSTOMER OBJECT at 200. A component mapping over an object renders an empty list and reports nothing, which is why it survived an audit. An unknown sub-resource 404s deliberately: falling through to the customer object is what made the defect invisible, so the fallback had to go. Served: `invoices`, `equipment`, `contracts`, `service-tickets`, `service-calls`, `supply-orders` (`customer_supply_orders`), `activities` (off `business_record_activities`, which keys on `business_record_id` - a customer IS a business record here), plus `financial-summary`, `aging` and `payments` DERIVED from the invoice set because PostgREST has no SUM. Rows go through `toCamel`. **There is no `payments` table**: payment history is derived from invoices carrying an `amount_paid`, so the response is `{ payments, unbacked }` and method/cheque number are null rather than blank; `averagePaymentDays` is null when nothing has settled and `creditLimit`/`availableCredit` are null when the record carries none. **CORRECTED 2026-08-30 (PA-021): `/api/customers` IS in `crmProxies` now, as a plain `'customers'` entry, and both special cases are gone.** The old note said the bare list must stay special-cased to `companies` because a proxy entry would send it to a function answering a different shape. The shapes do differ - `companies` returns `{data,total,page,limit}`, `customers` a bare array that iOS decodes as `[BusinessRecord]` - but PRODUCTION ALREADY SENT THE LIST TO `customers` and cannot do otherwise, since `getApiUrl` rewrites the prefix straight to the functions host. So the special case preserved a dev-only shape no deployed client saw, and both functions read the same `companies` table anyway. Every web consumer goes through `extractRecords`, which tolerates either. The `app.get('/api/customers/:id/:sub')` forward is gone too, and its own defect is the reason: it matched exactly THREE segments, so `/api/customers/:id/metrics/history` fell through to Express while `/devices` did not - two tabs of the Customer Detail page on two different backends. Three more things this exposed. `handleSubResource` was dispatched only for GET, so `POST /customers/:id/supply-orders` fell into the create-CUSTOMER branch and wrote a junk `companies` row from the order payload at 201 while the UI said "Supply order created successfully" - production was the only host where that button did anything. `customer_supply_orders` is an order HEADER (`order_number` NOT NULL UNIQUE, `delivery_address` jsonb NOT NULL, `customer_portal_user_id` NOT NULL to a portal login) with lines in `customer_supply_order_items`, so the flat `{supplyId,quantity,unitPrice,totalPrice,orderType,notes}` the dialog posted is unwritable; that path answers 501 and the dialog is deleted. And both Express handlers behind the meter-readings tab filtered `device_registrations` by `tenant_id` ONLY, despite `device_registrations.customer_id` existing, so every customer's tab showed the whole tenant's fleet - real numbers about the wrong customers. Thirteen shadowed Express handlers went with it (`routes-customers.ts` entirely, plus handlers in `routes-crm-core`, `routes-business-records`, `routes-enhanced-service` - which mounts at the `/api` ROOT, so its bare `'/customers'` is under this prefix - and `routes-workflow-mobile`); the fourteenth is a SEC-SESSION-001 retention. One casualty worth knowing: `server/lib/custom-field-validation.ts` is now unreachable, so nothing validates custom-field VALUES on a write (the `custom-fields` edge fn validates DEFINITIONS, which is different) - kept and baselined rather than deleted so the gap stays on the roadmap. Nine of these calls were also RAW `fetch()`, already sitting in `docs/raw-api-fetch-baseline.json`, and nobody had connected the two entries; one defect hid inside the other.

A QUERY STRING HID NINE DEAD BUTTONS FROM check:nav (AUDIT-014). `isNoise()` discards any literal containing a regex character and `?` is one, so EVERY navigation target carrying a query string was thrown away before it was checked - the exact false negative that guard's header says it exists to avoid. `pathOf()` now strips `?query` and `#fragment` first (only from something already shaped like a path, so a real regex fragment still reaches `isNoise` intact), and that immediately surfaced three LIVE 404s nothing had ever reported: `/activities` twice in the sales-rep dashboard widgets and `/proposals/new` in `LeadDeals`. **Do not narrow the collect-every-slash-literal design** - its header records a post-login 404 that survived a whole audit pass because an earlier script only matched navigation expressions, and it is what found the two dead redirect whitelists below. My first instinct was to narrow it; the header talked me out of it.

`?action=` WAS READ BY NOTHING. `main-layout` renders `SmartBreadcrumb` on every page and its quick actions linked to `<list page>?action=new`; `grep searchParams.get('action')` returned nothing, so "Add Equipment", "Generate Invoice", "Submit Meter Reading" and six more dropped the user on a list and did nothing. `client/src/hooks/use-action-param.ts` reads it and STRIPS IT with `history.replaceState`, so a refresh or a back-press does not reopen the dialog; six pages open their create dialog from it. Three controls were DELETED rather than repointed, per AUDIT-016's rule: "Log Call" (x2) pointed at `/activities` and there is no sales-rep activities surface (`PlatformActivities` is root-admin only), and "Transfer Parts" pointed at a feature `WarehouseOperations` does not have - the word does not appear in the page.

A COMMENTED-OUT SECURITY CHECK PLUS A STALE LIST IS WORSE THAN NEITHER (AUDIT-014). `auth-utils.ts`'s `ALLOWED_REDIRECT_PATHS` and `validations.ts`'s `ALLOWED_REDIRECT_PREFIXES` were both declared with enforcement commented out, and both had gone stale - most entries (`/dashboard`, `/analytics`, `/profile`, `/reports`, `/settings`) are not registered routes, so switching either on would have sent users to the 404 page after login. Both deleted; that was 14 of the 15 nav-baseline entries. The control that actually closes open redirect stays: `sanitizeURL`, `BLOCKED_REDIRECT_PREFIXES`, and `isValidRedirect`'s same-origin relative-path requirement. An allow-list of ROUTES would only stop a user reaching a valid-but-unintended page, which is not a security boundary.

THE SERVER NOW HAS A REACHABILITY WALK TOO (AUDIT-034). `npm run check:server-orphans` is the counterpart to `check:orphan-files`, walking from `server/index.ts` through every static AND dynamic import - including `routes-registry`'s table-driven `await import(variable)` mounts, where the specifier is a variable and a scan for `import('` finds none of them, so any relative string literal in a file containing a dynamic import counts. Baseline **151** (13 middleware, 39 services, 17 routers), a TODO list. Build it into your reasoning rather than grepping for importers: **reachability is transitive**, and one-level grep gets this wrong in both directions. `warehouse-reporting-service.ts` HAS an importer and that importer has none, so it is dead. `session-timeout.ts` has an importer that IS live and it is correctly not reported - but that importer takes only the helper functions, so its enforcement middleware is still never mounted. **File-reachable is not middleware-mounted**, and no walk can tell you the second thing.

THREE SECURITY CONTROLS WERE WRITTEN AND APPLIED BY NOTHING, AND NONE OF THEM COULD HAVE BEEN TURNED ON (AUDIT-034). The `security-index.ts` barrel (now deleted) was the only place `enforceSessionTimeout`, `extendSessionOnActivity`, `enforceIpWhitelist`, `requireMfaForAdmins` and `requireMfaVerification` were ever applied, and nothing imported that file - so sessions never timed out, no IP whitelist was enforced, and no privileged user was ever required to hold MFA. **The story filed this as wanting "a real database and a rollout order", and reading the three found that each is blocked on a prerequisite that does not exist**, which is a different and cheaper answer than a rollout. All three are DELETED; `server/tests/unit/server-orphans.test.ts` now asserts each prerequisite is still absent, so it FAILS the day one lands rather than going stale. **Session idle timeout could not fire**: `isSessionExpired` returns `{expired:false}` without `session.sessionMetadata`, and the only thing that set it - `initializeSession` - had no callers, so mounting it was a no-op rather than a pending rollout; its guard was `req.session.userId`, which the product's real login never sets (the web app uses `supabase.auth.signInWithPassword`, and the two handlers that do set it sit under `/api/auth`, which production resolves to an edge directory that does not exist); express-session already caps the cookie at 24h; and the expiry branch cleared `connect.sid` while this app names its cookie `sid`. **IP whitelist could not be enabled**: `getSecuritySettings` fetches the compliance row and DISCARDS it, returning `{...DEFAULT_SECURITY_SETTINGS}` with `ipWhitelistEnabled: false` under its own "For now, return defaults" comment - and a Postgres 16 with all 80 journalled migrations replayed has **no `ip_whitelist%` column on any of its 682 tables and no `security_settings` table at all**. **MFA-for-admins is the one that WOULD have run, and would have locked everyone out**: this file used to say it 403s users at level 4+ "subject to per-tenant settings", and **there are no per-tenant settings** - `compliance_settings` carries no `mfa` column (measured on the same replayed chain), so `settings?.mfaEnabled !== false` was `undefined !== false`, true for every tenant, configured or not. Three more, each sufficient on its own: its only policy writer (`supabase/functions/admin/`) maps nine fields and none is an MFA field, and has no caller either; two of the four paths `applySecurityMiddleware` targeted (`/api/security`, `/api/users/bulk`) are PROXIED, so Express never sees them; and `pathRequiresMfa(req.path)` can never match, because `app.use(path, mw)` strips the mount prefix. `apiVersioning` and `legacyRouteSupport` ARE mounted, directly in `server/routes.ts` and never through that module, so versioning works - do not overstate the finding. **WHAT THE DELETION LEAVES OPEN, and it is a real gap**: nothing requires an admin to hold MFA, and step-up MFA for sensitive actions is unimplemented on the Express side - `requireMFA` in `enhanced-rbac-middleware.ts` survives, fails closed on `req.session.mfaVerified`, and its only writer (`markMfaVerified`) went with `mfa-enforcement.ts`, so it now 403s every caller including one who has just enrolled. It is kept, inert and with no mount site, under a header saying exactly that. It is also the wrong layer: enrolment lives in `supabase/functions/mfa/` over `mfa_enrollments` and `/api/mfa` is proxied, while `storage.getUserMfaStatus` reads `user_settings.two_factor_enabled` - **two stores for one fact**, which is the question to settle before anything is mounted again. THE METHOD WORTH REUSING: for a control nobody mounted, do not ask whether to mount it - ask what it would READ, and go and look for that column. Twice here the answer was a column that does not exist, and once it was a policy store with no writer.

A PORT CAN TIDY A MOCK INSTEAD OF REMOVING IT, AND THAT IS WORSE (AUDIT-021). `supabase/functions/customer-portal/handlers/analytics.ts` serves the customer portal's usage analytics on BOTH hosts (`/api/customer-portal` is proxied), and its comparison block never queried a previous period: it multiplied the current one by **0.9** and called the result "previous", so every customer on every render was told their volume was up 11.1% and their cost up 11.1%, feeding all four trend cards. That constant was a deliberate port of the Express version's random 0.85-1.15 multiplier, and the header called it an improvement because "deterministic responses cache better" - the exact inversion of AUDIT-020's finding, which is that a STABLE fabrication is harder to spot than a random one because refreshing appears to confirm it. Peak usage was hardcoded the same way (9am 120 pages, Tuesday 520) and presented to each customer as their own pattern. The fix: one fetch spanning both windows, split on `startDate`, with a single `summarise()` run over each side so the two halves cannot drift; `percentageChange` null when the previous window has no readings, since a customer with one month of history has no trend and 0% asserts that nothing changed; day and month peaks derived from the readings and AVERAGED per weekday rather than summed, so a longer window does not skew them. Hourly is ABSENT - a meter submission carries a `reading_date` and no time of day, so no arithmetic over this data produces an hourly profile - with the chart removed and the gap named in the response's `unbacked` array, alongside two more worth knowing: the cost figures use an industry-average rate card rather than the customer's contract rates, and the per-device `costPerPage`/`efficiency` are fleet-wide estimates. `MetricCard` now takes `number | null` and renders nothing rather than "null%" (it also printed `11.111111111111114%` unrounded). The Express copy of all of this - 669 lines, `getUsageAnalytics` plus nine private helpers - was deleted: nothing routed it.

A PHANTOM-TABLE FINDING CAN POINT AT A WHOLE DEAD LAYER (AUDIT-024). The twelve live-service entries `check:sql-string-tables` reported (`activities`, `quotas`, `sales_quotas`, `parts_usage`, `ai_tasks`) turned out to belong to TEN `server/services/*-reporting-service.ts` files, 5,839 lines, that **nothing imported** - and `supabase/functions/reports/` says why in its own handler headers: "Replaces `server/routes/<x>-reports-api.ts` + `server/services/<x>-reporting-service.ts`". The Express routers were retired; their service layer was not. Eight are deleted. The live implementation is also strictly better, which is what made that safe rather than merely tidy: its `_queries/` modules build every statement through PostgREST's builder, so there is no interpolation to audit, and its headers record the unbacked tables honestly ("sales_quotas table does NOT exist -> personal quota report is degraded") instead of querying them. THE METHOD, worth reusing: grep every tree for an importer, THEN look for a comment in the replacement naming the file - the second half is what turns "nobody calls this" into "this was deliberately superseded and nobody finished the job". Two survive and are annotated rather than deleted (`warehouse`, `service-supervisor`), because `server/services/team-alert-service.ts` calls their `getTeamQuickStats` and that service is itself unreachable over four real tables - PROD-008c's shape, so all three files carry a header saying so. Separately, `performance-monitor.ts`'s `getSlowQueries()` returned two hand-written rows (a 1250ms query against `ai_tasks`, an 890ms one against `calendar_events`) in a function whose siblings had already been corrected to answer null rather than guess, and the caller branches on `slowQueries.length > 5`, so the fiction fed a recommendation too; it now returns `[]` beside a real `slowQueryCount` from `db-logger`.

AN ENDPOINT CAN BE A SEEDER IN DISGUISE (CR-017). `POST /api/mobile/sync` looped over users whose role matched `'%technician%'` and INSERTed a fabricated GPS position for each - `40.7128 + Math.random() * 0.1`, a point in New York, with a random 80-100% battery - into `technician_locations`, a REAL table, then answered "Mobile data sync completed" with a count. `check:no-random-metrics` did not see it because that guard watches an object PROPERTY assigned from `Math.random()`, not a value passed positionally into a query. Its sibling `GET /api/mobile-field/metrics` ended with `SELECT 95.5 as gps_accuracy`, commented "Mock GPS accuracy metric". Both are gone. What remains on that surface is AUDIT-033: `/api/mobile/dashboard` is a FIXTURE with eight callers including the native app's dashboard screen (1247 completed jobs, a 4.8 rating, $2,340.50 of revenue today), and `MobileFieldOperations.tsx` - 1507 lines over three tables that never existed - now 404s on both hosts rather than 500ing on one.

TWO EXPRESS ROUTERS CAN OWN ONE PREFIX, AND MOUNT ORDER PICKS THE WINNER (CR-017). `/api/commission` had `routes-operations-extended` at `routes-registry:359` and `routes-commission` at `:844`, so the first won `/calculations` and `/disputes` (over the phantom tables above) and the second's copies of those two never ran, while its `/plans` and `/analytics` did - and those returned a hardcoded "Sales Rep Standard" plan with 5%/6.5%/8% tiers. Production ran neither: `getApiUrl` sends the prefix to `supabase/functions/commission/`, which covers every path the page calls. Both routers are deleted and the prefix is proxied. `POST /api/commission/calculate` had no Express handler under either router, so it worked in production only. **The worse half was in the edge function**: its `GET /calculations` recomputed pay from won deals at a flat "simplified - 5% base rate" plus a $2,500 bonus over $100,000 and returned it at 200 - invented pay is harder to spot when it reads real deals to get there, and no plan, tier or product rate was consulted. Three hundred lines below, `POST /calculate` already answered 501 saying the engine that writes `commission_calculations` does not exist. The read branch now reads that table (plus `commission_calculation_details`, `commission_bonuses`, `commission_adjustments` - note the first two have NO `tenant_id` and hang off `calculation_id`), so the two agree and an unbuilt engine shows an empty list.

A PREFIX WORD CAN BELONG TO TWO DOMAINS, AND THE COLLISION IS SILENT (AUDIT-031). `FleetMonitoringDashboard` called `/api/fleet/dashboard` for a PRINTER fleet. `/api/fleet` is not proxied, so dev was served by three real Express handlers in `routes-client-monitoring.ts` over `device_registrations` + `device_metrics` and the page looked correct. Production resolves segment 0 to `supabase/functions/fleet/`, which is about VEHICLES - `fleet_vehicles`, `fleet_maintenance`, `fleet_fuel_logs` - so `/dashboard` answered **200** with `totalVehicles`/`availableVehicles` under keys the page does not read and every card fell back to `|| 0`, while `/devices` 404'd. A 200 of the wrong domain beats a 404 for damage: nothing throws, nothing logs, and the screen is quietly all zeroes. `check:edge-coverage` does not see it either, because the segment IS served. Before adding a page under an existing prefix word, open the edge function directory of that name and read what it queries. The endpoints moved to `/api/device-monitoring`, the proxied function that already owns those four tables. Two things this exposed: `supabase/functions/fleet/` has no caller in any client tree at all (the printer page's mistaken reference was the only thing making `check:unreferenced-edge-fns` pass on it - baselined 84 -> 85, and it is the cleanest AUDIT-024 instance), and its four tables are in no Drizzle schema. Third, unrelated defect on the same page: Order Toner posted `device_id`/`order_type`/`items`/`requested_by` into `supply_orders`, which has none of those columns (`machine_id` NOT NULL, `color`, `part_number`, `quantity`, `created_by_user_id`), so it was always a PGRST204 reported as "Failed to create toner order" - it now writes `device_supply_orders`, one row per colour.

A QUERY KEY IS A URL. `getQueryFn` does `queryKey.join('/')`, so `['/api/x/devices', searchQuery]` requests `/api/x/devices/<term>` and the term is read as an id. The page had done this since it was written and had always filtered client-side; the server-side search it appeared to ask for never existed on either host. Only put path segments in a query key - a filter value goes in the URL's query string or nowhere. It cuts the other way too (PROD-010): `BusinessProcessOptimization` and `IncidentResponseSystem`, 865 and 1162 lines, both passed filter state in the key (`['/api/business-process/dashboard', selectedCategory, selectedDepartment]`), so both requested `/dashboard/all/all`, 404'd in dev as well as prod, and rendered nothing behind `{data && ...}`. Both were backed by hardcoded Express handlers with zero db calls - 47 processes, $127,890.50 saved, 7 active incidents, threat feeds, IOC matches - so the defect is the only reason those figures never shipped, and anyone "fixing" the key would have published them. Both are deleted, along with the unregistered 746-line `routes-business-process-optimization.ts`, and `missingEdge` in `docs/route-ownership-baseline.json` is now **0**. So a broken query key can HIDE a fabricated backend as easily as it breaks a working one, and the three fabrication guards all watch the render path: check what URL a dashboard's key actually produces before porting or repairing it.

Do NOT add a `crmProxies` entry for an un-migrated prefix: the proxy forwards the whole
prefix and falls through only on a NETWORK error, never a 404 — so proxying a broken fn
takes it from working-in-dev to 404-in-dev too.

Counting caveat: grepping for `pathParts[1]` alone over-reports ~48x — it is CORRECT in
functions that read `pathParts[0]` as the resource (post-strip) and `[1]` as an id, e.g.
`deals` (`dealId = pathParts[0]`) and `contacts`. The real signature is a function that
reads `[1]` but NEVER `[0]`.

Fabricated operational values (AUDIT-019): three guards cover three different shapes and none of them sees the other two. `check:no-mocks` catches a mock IDENTIFIER (`generateMockX`, `const mockFoo = [...]`) or a mock returned from a catch block, scoped to CRM/sales. `check:fabricated` catches a read off query data OR'd with a plausible literal (`revenueMetrics?.mrr || 89000`). `check:no-static-posture` (new, hard gate at zero, `scripts/check-no-static-posture.mjs`, CI-wired) catches the third: a page with NO query at all, whose every value is written inline in the JSX. It works on the SHAPE of the rendered slot rather than the name of the source: a bare value as a JSX text node (currency, unit, ratio, decimal, or two-plus digits), a posture word as a complete text node, a `<Progress value={NUMBER}>`, or a literal expiry date. A bare single digit is excluded deliberately: ordered-step markers are labels, not measurements. Scope WAS `client/src/pages/admin` plus four named pages; PA-040 widened it to all of `client/src/pages` (baseline 42, marketing excluded by rule) - see the guard-scope note above before quoting the old scope. What it found beyond SystemSecurity (442 lines, zero dynamic values, asserting a TLS certificate valid until a date eight months in the past, still rendered green): RootAdminSecurity's privileged-role census, TenantManagement's $284,750 MRR on the platform billing screen, UserManagement's 45,789 monthly active users. The rule these all resolve to is the one AUDIT-016 and LEGAL-010 set: delete a claim with no backing data rather than fake it, and say plainly what is not measured so an absence is not read as an all-clear. `/api/admin/system-health` is the real signal source, and it lives ONLY in the admin edge fn, and the scoped proxy entry `'/api/admin/system-health'` is what makes it resolve in dev without taking the whole `/api/admin` prefix (routes-admin-workflows) off Express.

BACK ENDS WITH NO CALLER ARE THE COMMONEST DEFECT SHAPE IN THIS REPO (AUDIT-024). **AND THE NEXT LAYER IN IS AN ENGINE NOBODY FIRES INSIDE A FUNCTION THAT HAS PLENTY OF CALLERS (COP-B04).** The opportunity radar's scan was chunked, idempotent, kill-switched, ranked and territory-scoped, and `POST /scan` was fired by ONE BUTTON on its page and by nothing else, so a play about a lease ending in 90 days was detected the day a rep happened to look. `check:unreferenced-edge-fns` is blind to it - the function is referenced. Ask instead what FIRES a sweep, a recalculation or a digest: if the answer is a button, the feature is a report. `drizzle/cron/*.sql` is where a schedule goes, its README carries a job inventory (a file missing from that table is how a job stops being known to exist - `booking-reminders` sat unlisted for three days), and four rules make a multi-tenant sweep safe: `isCronRequest` is the WHOLE authentication with no user fallback, because a sweep across every tenant is not a user action; the cron branch sits ABOVE `auth.getUser`, since pg_cron carries no user JWT (the ordering defect INTEG-WEBHOOK-001 found on the webhook receiver); tenants run SEQUENTIALLY, because each one already pages its whole installed base; and a tenant that throws is recorded and stepped over, with an all-failed sweep answering 500 rather than a 200 nobody reads. Extract the per-tenant work into ONE function the button and the schedule share, or they drift and only one of them gets looked at. Four instances turned up in one session - CRMX-016's booking surface, PROD-008c's billing, PROD-008d's document automation, AUDIT-023's print-cost calculator - and no existing guard saw any of them, because `check:orphans` watches pages, `check:edge-coverage` watches paths, and neither watches a whole function nobody calls. `check:unreferenced-edge-fns` now does: baseline 96 of 285. A function counts as reachable when a client file names `/api/<its-directory-name>`, when it is a `crmProxies` `{fn, pathPrefix}` alias target, or when `server.ts` maps a segment onto it - that is the full set of routes from a browser, since `getApiUrl` rewrites `/api/<seg>` straight to the functions host in prod. pg_cron IS A CALLER: `drizzle/cron/*.sql` posts to edge functions via `pg_net.http_post`, and `email-marketing` and `field-service` are reached only that way. (`_shared/cron-auth.ts` is the helper a scheduled handler should use to verify the internal cron token instead of a user JWT - nothing imports it, which is worth a look on its own.) THIS REPO HAS SEVEN CLIENT TREES and the script walks all of them - `client/src`, `printyx-client`, `printyx-desktop`, `mobile-app`, `mobile`, `browser-extensions`, `printyx-extension`, `ios` - matching source files only, because a path named in a `SECURITY.md` is documentation, not a caller. Reading `client/src` alone reported five false positives, and I wrote one of them up as a dead duplicate before the iOS app turned out to call it (`today-dashboard`, `APIEndpoint.swift:305`; the web screen uses the `dashboards` function's `/today` branch, so it is a convergence question, not a deletion). The baseline is a TODO list; several entries (chrome-extension, oauth-proxy, signup, csrf-token, hello, db-probe) are legitimately headless and want annotating, not deleting.

WIRING A CALLER IS WHAT MAKES A BASELINED PHANTOM COLUMN A DEFECT (COP-B03). `supabase/functions/sales-territories/index.ts` named seven columns that do not exist on `sales_territories` - `name`, `region`, `states`, `zip_codes`, `rules`, `assigned_rep_id`, `created_by` - so every CRUD branch was a guaranteed 42703, the list endpoint included. TWO BASELINES HELD COMPLEMENTARY HALVES OF ONE FACT and both were true for the same reason: the columns sat in `docs/phantom-columns-baseline.json` and the whole function sat in `docs/unreferenced-edge-fns-baseline.json`. Nobody could reach it, so nothing was broken. COP-B09 then wired a page to it and shipped, and the defect only surfaced when the NEXT story tightened the unreferenced baseline and `server/tests/unit/phantom-cols-reachable-zero.test.ts` failed - that test exists precisely to assert the baseline holds nothing a caller can reach, and it is the reason this was a one-day gap rather than a permanent one. The rule to carry: when you give an unreferenced edge function its first caller, run `check:phantom-cols` against THAT function before believing the page works, and tighten the unreferenced baseline in the same commit so the reachability assertion is evaluated. The fix also shows what a partial PUT should do - write only the fields the caller sent, since a blanket object nulls every column a partial form omits - and that `territory_type` is NOT NULL with no database default, so a create must carry one.

A CONFIGURABLE PAGE IS A CONTRACT BETWEEN THREE STRINGS, AND TSC SEES NONE OF THEM (CRM-008). **A FOURTH STRING JOINS THEM WHEN THE PAGE NORMALISES ITS OWN RECORD.** The leads layout named `estimatedAmount` and `leadSource` - the DRIZZLE field names - while `GET /leads/:id` returns the raw row (the one read path in that function with no `toCamel`) and LeadDetail normalises it to `estimatedDealValue` and `source`, so both fields resolved to nothing. The contract is between the layout and the PAGE'S NORMALIZER, not between the layout and the schema. `server/tests/unit/record-layout-wiring.test.ts` now asserts every leads field against the normalizer's emitted keys and every EDITABLE one against what `_shared/business-record-write.ts` can store. Two smaller things that rewrite exposed and both generalise: a normalizer built on `||` sends a real 0 (a lead score, an estimated value) to the undefined camelCase key and renders it blank, so use `??`; and a catch reading `error.response?.data?.message` is always undefined here, because `apiRequest` throws a plain `Error` - every failure showed the generic fallback instead of the server's reason. A layout section is a string, a property field is a string, and the field name a write path accepts is a string in a map. `shared/record-layout.ts` + `client/src/components/crm/RecordPageLayout.tsx` is the engine; `server/tests/unit/record-layout-wiring.test.ts` locks the two joints, because both were already broken. FIRST: five fields the shipped deal layout makes editable - `nextFollowUpDate`, `primaryContactName`, `primaryContactEmail`, `primaryContactPhone`, `productsInterested` - are real columns read all over the tree and were absent from the deals PATCH field map, so a PATCH setting any of them answered **200 having changed nothing**. SECOND: a section the layout names and the page supplies no slot for renders as nothing at all, so the engine reports it in `unrenderable` and the page prints it rather than quietly showing an admin less than they configured. THREE RULES the engine encodes, each worth reusing wherever stored config drives a UI: a stored layout is authoritative but NOT total (a section shipped since it was saved is appended, or releasing a new section makes it invisible to every tenant that ever customised one); an unrenderable section is reported, never swallowed; and a configured field the record does not carry is dropped AND named, since a renamed column otherwise renders as blank rows that read as "no data" rather than "stale layout". The same pass closed an AUDIT-028 instance of the shape that note says the guard cannot see: `deal_activities` had exactly one writer in the tree and NO CALLER, so the deal timeline was a correct read of a table nothing could fill - the new compose area is that caller.

AN AC CAN BE HALF DONE ACROSS TWO PAGES AND READ AS SATISFIED FROM EITHER ONE (CRM-008 AC8, round 56). DealDetail has had the stage picker since CRM-008 shipped; LeadDetail rendered `status` as a read-only `<Badge>`, so **a rep could not advance a lead from its own record page** - they had to find it on a board. Nothing flags this: AC10 ("use RecordPageLayout") was genuinely done on both pages, the engine exports `RecordStageBar`, and opening either page alone tells you the AC holds. When a story names two surfaces, check the capability on BOTH; the shared component existing is not the same as both callers using it. `LeadDetail` now asks `GET /api/sales-pipeline/stages` for the vocabulary rather than carrying a list, because COP-E02's defect was exactly a page and a board holding two different stage models (`pipeline_stages.id` is a uuid, `business_records.status` is a lifecycle word, and `findIndex` across them yields -1 which `+ 1` turns into index 0). **A status outside the vocabulary is APPENDED to the bar, not dropped**: an unknown value leaves nothing highlighted, which reads as "not started" rather than "this word is not one of ours". The write reuses the page's existing `saveField`, so it inherits COP-M01's `ignoredFields`/`refusedFields` reporting instead of introducing a second write path.

TWO CLAUDE.md CLAIMS CORRECTED BY READING THE TREE (round 56). COP-M01's note said "LeadDetail.tsx hand-rolls ~1400 lines of form... DealDetail is wired to RecordPageLayout while LeadDetail is not" - LeadDetail is 578 lines, renders through the engine, and its own header says so. And the CRM-008 entry above warns that `/api/record-layout-config` "404s in production and runs for nobody in dev" - `supabase/functions/record-layout-config/` exists and the prefix is in `crmProxies`, so it resolves on both hosts now. That is the seventh and eighth stale claim this session. The rule stands and keeps earning: **`ls` the file, grep the column, run the guard, before building on any note in here.**

A COLUMN REGISTRY IS A PROMISE ABOUT AN ENDPOINT, AND NOTHING CHECKS IT (COP-M01). `client/src/lib/crm-object-registry.ts` drives the CRM list tables: `fields` are columns, `defaultColumns` picks which show, `quickFilters[].serverKey` becomes a query parameter. The leads entry was written against `business_records` while its own `apiEndpoint` reads `companies` - COP-B00's open contradiction - so **four of the seven default columns could never hold a value**: `priority`, `leadSource` and `estimatedAmount` are columns on no table the endpoint touches, and `createdAt` existed but the mapper emitted it in snake_case only while every other field in that mapper emits both spellings. Two of the four were marked `editable`, so inline-editing them posted a field the write path cannot store. **Worse than a blank column: a quick filter that does nothing.** The handler reads `limit`, `offset`, `ownerId`, `recordType`, `scope`, `search` and `status` and nothing else, so the Priority and Source filters sent a parameter it ignores and the list came back unchanged - a control that appears to work beats a missing one for damage, and this is PA-040's key-name shape on the REQUEST side rather than the response. The unbacked columns and filters are REMOVED, not left in the column picker: an empty column a user can choose reads as "no data yet" rather than "this does not exist". `server/tests/unit/crm-shell-nav-parity.test.ts` derives both checks rather than pinning a list - every `defaultColumns` entry must be a key the response mapper emits, and every `serverKey` must be a parameter the handler reads. Before repointing any nav entry at a registry-driven page, run that comparison for the object.

A SORT WHITELIST IS THE FOURTH STRING IN THAT CONTRACT (COP-I01 AC4, round 107). The registry names a field, the mapper emits a key, the write path accepts a column - and the sort spec has to resolve the same name the REGISTRY sends, which is the MAPPER's vocabulary and not the table's. AC4 claimed "server-side pagination, filtering and sorting on every CRM index endpoint" and was two-thirds true: **the leads index read no sort parameter at all**, issuing a hardcoded `.order('created_at', ...)` while `CrmDataTable` sends `sortBy`/`sortOrder` on every request, so all seven column headers the registry marked sortable did nothing - the arrow flipped, a request went out carrying the sort, the same page came back. It also had no LIMIT CLAMP, doing `parseInt(get('limit') || '100')` by hand on the one CRM endpoint every other list has clamped to `MAX_CRM_PAGE_SIZE` since COP-I01 shipped. **Checking the rest of the family found a second**: the deals index marked `stage` sortable against a spec whose entry is `stageId`, so one header of nineteen fell back silently. **The obvious fix was the trap**: both handlers read `companies`, so reusing `COMPANY_LIST_SPEC` looks right and falls back on three of five (`companyName` vs businessName, `status` vs activity, `city` vs billingCity). And **a field with no column to sort by is `sortable: false`, never a whitelist entry pointing at nothing** - `primaryContactName`/`primaryContactEmail` come from an embedded relation and PostgREST cannot order a parent by an embedded column, and sorting deals by `stage_id` is ordering by a uuid.

THREE SELF-INFLICTED TRAPS IN ONE ROUND, each one this file already records (round 107). **Two copies of a spec drift and the EDGE one is what production runs**: mutating the Deno copy's `companyName` mapping left every test green, because the new tests import the NODE copy - `crm-list-query-parity.test.ts` exists to stop exactly that and I had added a spec to both twins without extending it. Adding a spec means adding its parity block. **A line-anchored walk misses a one-line object**: requiring `\n\s+sortable: true` found two of the five leads fields, because three are written as `{ field: 'city', ..., sortable: true, ... }` - the blind spot `check:no-random-metrics` names in its own header, reproduced from scratch. And **the comment trap, eighth occurrence, inside a comment written minutes earlier**: the annotation explaining why `stage` is no longer sortable QUOTES `sortable: true`, so the walk reported the explanation as the defect.

DO NOT RUN TWO tsc-INVOKING GUARDS AT ONCE (round 107). `check:throwing-types` failed in a background sweep and passed alone: it and `scripts/check-types.mjs` both shell out to tsc, and `tsconfig.json` sets `incremental: true` with `tsBuildInfoFile` under `node_modules/typescript`, so two concurrent runs share one cache file.
