The earlier "`@sentry/react` missing from node_modules" note was a **failed install**, not a repo defect: `engines.node` is `20.x` with `engine-strict=true` in `.npmrc`, so on any other Node major `npm install` aborts and leaves node_modules EMPTY. Under Node 20 the dependency is present and the build succeeds. The baseline quoted below also drifted — the real count is now **1067**, not 3577. If a command fails inexplicably, run `npm run check:node` FIRST; `preinstall` now enforces this and `scripts/check-types.mjs` refuses to compare a count when tsc never actually typechecked (it used to report "errors DECREASED" and exit 0 on an empty tree, which would have silently retired the ratchet). (2) High-leverage burn-down pattern: when many TS7006 implicit-any callback params cluster in one file, the data source is usually an untyped `apiRequest()` useQuery — type the derived `useMemo`/array with the file's EXISTING domain interface (e.g. `useMemo<EquipmentHealth[]>`) and the type flows through every `.find/.reduce/.filter/.map` callback at once, zero runtime change. CORRECTED 2026-08-27: this line used to say `server/routes-predictive-service-dispatch.ts` (~69 errors, phantom `serviceCallsEnhanced`/`equipmentMetrics`/`technicianResourcesEnhanced` tables, 500 at runtime) was DEFERRED. That file no longer exists — CR-017 deleted the stale Express router, and `/api/predictive-dispatch` is proxied to `supabase/functions/predictive-dispatch/`, which was already serving production. Nothing is deferred here and there are no 69 errors to budget for; do not go looking for the file. (3) PHANTOM-SHAPE PAGES (BATCH 7, VendorManagement.tsx, 54 errs->0): a dense TS2339+TS2322 cluster on a CRUD page is often the page reading/writing field names that DON'T EXIST on the real Drizzle table — built against an old/mock shape. The tell: errors say "Property X does not exist on type {id; ...realcols}". These are LATENT RUNTIME BUGS (blank list cells, create POSTs the DB silently drops), so the fix is to re-bind every field to the REAL columns (confirm via shared/schema.ts + migration 0000 DDL + the storage method the /api route actually calls) and DELETE features with no backing column (VendorManagement's "preferred" had no column — removed it, not faked it). Two recurring sub-fixes: derive the form type from `insertXSchema.omit({tenantId:true})` (not bare `z.infer` that keeps server-injected cols / re-omits already-omitted createdAt/updatedAt -> TS2322 "never"), and add `value={field.value ?? ''}` to every Input/Textarea bound to a NULLABLE varchar (string|null is not a valid HTML value — this clears most of the TS2322s). (4) PHANTOM-SHAPE PAGES (BATCH 8, MeterReadings.tsx, 41 errs->0): same pattern, plus a normalization-layer tell. The page's useQuery `queryFn` already had a `.map((row:any)=>({...row, blackMeter: row.black_meter ...}))` normalizer inventing camelCase keys (blackMeter/colorMeter) that DON'T exist on the real Drizzle type — but the Express GET returns raw `SELECT *` snake_case rows, so against live data the page renders BLANK. Fix = make the normalizer emit the REAL camelCase column names (`bw_meter_reading`->`bwMeterReading`, `color_meter_reading`->`colorMeterReading`) and re-point every read/form field to them. Two extras beyond BATCH 7: (a) when the form omits BOTH `tenantId` AND `createdBy` (both server-injected, both NOT NULL), `insertXSchema.omit({tenantId:true,createdBy:true})` — a bare `.extend()` keeps them required and every defaultValues/field errors; (b) a phantom FK with no backing column (equipment has `locationDescription` free-text, NO `locationId`/locations FK) — don't fake a join; derive the filter options from the distinct free-text values present on the list (`Array.from(new Set(equipment.map(e=>e.locationDescription)))`), keeping the feature against real data. Server debt found but deferred: the POST route's `insertMeterReadingSchema.parse` requires `createdBy` it never injects (500s) — frontend posting correct column names is still a strict improvement. (5) UNTYPED-useQuery DATA-CONTRACT PAGE (BATCH 9, PlatformAnalytics.tsx, 32 errs->0): a TS2339-on-`{}` cluster (+ TS7006 on chart `.map` params) where `useQuery({queryKey})` passes NO generic, so `data` is `{}` and every `data?.foo` read errors. Distinct from BATCH 7/8 phantom-CRUD: this page reads FLAT keys (`revenueMetrics?.mrr`, `growthTrends?.revenueData`) with `|| mock` fallbacks on every field, but the `platform-analytics` edge fn's revenue-metrics returns a NESTED `{metrics,counts}` shape (**CORRECTED 2026-08-30, PA-040: only revenue-metrics did — conversion-metrics, pipeline-metrics, performance-metrics and growth-trends were always flat and always matched. It is flat now and the page's fallbacks are gone**) — so the reads resolve to undefined and the page silently shows MOCK data. Fix = type-only: add flat OPTIONAL interfaces matching the keys the page reads + chart-row types, apply `useQuery<T>(...)`; the fallback arrays and `.map` params then infer. Do NOT rewrite the flat<-nested contract here — that's a separate story (same gap EDGE-004 flagged for PlatformCustomerSuccess.tsx). General rule for these: when a page reads off an untyped useQuery, type the QUERY (one generic) rather than casting each read; the mock-fallback pattern means a flat-optional interface is both faithful to current runtime and zero-change. (6) PHANTOM-SHAPE PAGES (BATCH 10, Vendors.tsx, 30 errs->0): IDENTICAL to BATCH 7 — there are TWO vendor CRUD pages (VendorManagement.tsx fixed in B7, Vendors.tsx here) both built against the same phantom shape (vendorNumber/companyName/displayName/contactPerson/address/country/currency/vendorType/category/status/preferred + a banking section bankName/accountHolder/routingNumber/bankAccountNumber) absent from the real `vendors` table (real cols vendorName/primaryContactName/addressLine1/2/city/state/zipCode/phone/fax/email/website/paymentTerms/taxId/accountNumber/creditLimit/isActive/vendorNotes). Same fix recipe verbatim: form schema = `insertVendorSchema.omit({tenantId:true})`; `useQuery<Vendor[]>` (clears the TS18046 `vendors` is unknown that also blocks `.filter`); re-bind every field + card to real columns; `value={field.value ?? ''}` on nullable inputs; DELETE no-column features (here: vendorNumber/displayName/vendorType/currency/category, the Preferred Star toggle + its Star/StarOff lucide imports, the whole banking section), status-string select -> isActive boolean Switch, add a vendorNotes Textarea. LESSON: when you fix a phantom-shape page, grep for SIBLING pages on the same table (`grep -rl vendorNumber client/src`) — duplicate CRUD pages built off the same mock shape travel in pairs. (7) PHANTOM-SHAPE PAGES (BATCH 11, EnhancedProductAccessories.tsx, 35 errs->0): SAME pattern on the product_accessories CRUD page. The add/edit form bound 4 phantom fields (partNumber/weight/dimensions/warrantyPeriod) absent from the real `product_accessories` table (shared/schema.ts:2932; real cols accessoryCode/accessoryName/accessoryType/category/manufacturer/description + 3 pricing tiers + isActive/availableForAll/salesRepCredit/funding/lease), and the model-compatibility dialog read model.modelName/model.productType off `product_models` (real cols productName/category — NO modelName/productType). /api/product-accessories reads/writes the real Drizzle tables, so create POSTed dropped columns + compat dialog rendered blank model names (latent runtime bugs). Recipe verbatim: form schema = `insertProductAccessorySchema.omit({tenantId:true})` -> `AccessoryFormData` (the bare `InsertProductAccessory` kept server-injected tenantId, so defaultValues TS2353'd AND the zodResolver `Control<...>` generic mismatched at EVERY FormField — ~13 cascading TS2322 that all cleared from the ONE omit, not per-field fixes); `CompatibilityPayload = Omit<InsertAccessoryModelCompatibility,'tenantId'>` for the link-model mutation (cleared TS2345); useForm<AccessoryFormData>+zodResolver(accessoryFormSchema); mutation/onSubmit param types -> AccessoryFormData; REMOVED the 4 phantom FormFields; model.modelName->productName, dropped phantom productType (already fell back to model.category); `value={field.value ?? ''}` on every nullable Input/Textarea/Select; narrowed the manufacturer/accessoryType filter arrays with `.filter((m): m is string => Boolean(m))` (`filter(Boolean)` does NOT narrow string|null -> SelectItem value TS2322). Baseline 3638->3603 (-35). NEW TAKEAWAY beyond B7/10: a big chunk of the error count on these form pages is the resolver `Control<...>` cascade — one wrong useForm<T> generic detonates a TS2322 at every FormField, so the `.omit({tenantId:true})` form-schema fix is worth far more than its line count suggests. Product CRUD pages (vendors x2, meter readings, accessories) were ALL built against stale mock shapes — grep the real table columns FIRST. (8) UNTYPED-useQuery (BATCH 12, RemoteMonitoring.tsx, 26 errs->0): same as BATCH 9 — two `useQuery({queryKey})` calls (fleet-overview, sensor-data) with no generic typed `data` as `{}`, so every nested read (`.summary.*`, `.statusDistribution`, `.performanceTrends.weekly*`, `.topPerformers`, `.attentionRequired`, `.historicalData.*`, `.predictions.*`) was TS2339, plus 2 TS7006 on the `weeklyUptime.map((uptime,index))` chart-data params. Fix = add `FleetOverview`/`SensorData` interfaces matching the keys read and apply `useQuery<T>`; the chart `.map` params infer from `number[]`. Type-only, the `fleetOverview && /sensorData &&` guards already null-tolerate. Baseline 3603->3577.

Accessibility (CR-035): `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` runs as four ERROR rules in the `**/*.{ts,tsx}` block of `eslint.config.js` — `label-has-associated-control`, `click-events-have-key-events`, `no-static-element-interactions`, `anchor-is-valid` — all at zero repo-wide, `printyx-desktop` included. NOT `recommended`: its tail is false positives here (`heading-has-content`/`anchor-has-content` on shadcn primitives that forward children through `{...props}`, `no-autofocus` on search dialogs, `no-noninteractive-tabindex` on `FocusManager.tsx`). The rule OPTIONS are load-bearing — `controlComponents` naming the shadcn wrappers plus `depth: 3` clears 41 correctly-associated labels the rule cannot otherwise see through a wrapper component; removing them re-reports working code, it does not tighten anything. To make an existing `<div onClick>` keyboard-operable use `clickableProps` from `client/src/lib/accessibility` — its `event.target !== event.currentTarget` guard is the part that matters, because several of these wrappers contain inputs and a space typed inside one otherwise bubbles up, gets `preventDefault`ed and never reaches the field. To associate a label, PREFER WRAPPING (the `<div>` holding the control becomes the `<label>`, the inner `<label>` becomes a `<span>`) over `htmlFor`: no id means nothing collides when a create and an edit dialog are both mounted. A `<label>` with no control at all is a caption — make it a `<span>`. Two classes jsx-a11y does NOT cover and `npm run check:a11y-names` hard-gates at zero: icon-only `<Button>` (no rule fires, because the button has a child) and search `<Input>` named only by a placeholder. That guard reads JSX as text, so a name assembled at runtime or an icon rendered through a variable is invisible to it; the 173 non-search placeholder-only inputs are deliberately out of its scope, since browsers do fall back to the placeholder and the real fix is a visible label.

AN OVERRIDE BUILT FOR A ROLE NOBODY CHECKS IS NOT AN OVERRIDE (SEC-EDGE-001). Every `customer-portal` branch resolved its customer as `?customerId=` || `?customer_id=` || the JWT claim, then checked only that the customer belonged to the caller's tenant - on the SERVICE ROLE client, which bypasses RLS. The parameter exists for a real feature (`_context.ts` called it the dealer-staff view) but **nothing verified the caller was staff**, and a portal customer is in the tenant too, so `?customerId=<sibling>` returned the sibling's dashboard, invoices, equipment and usage. Five branches each carried their own copy of that chain, plus `resolveCustomerId` for the analytics handlers, so a sixth would have inherited it. Fixed by resolving ONCE after the tenant resolve and having every branch read the answer; a claim-holder asking for a different id gets **403 rather than a silent fallback to their own data**, because quietly serving the right rows hides a probe, and a caller with no claim is staff and keeps the override. `resolveCustomerId` stays a function returning `ctx.customerId` so a handler going back to local resolution shows up in a diff.

**WHEN A FILE EXPLAINS WHY ONE IDENTITY FIELD CANNOT BE TRUSTED, CHECK EVERY OTHER IDENTITY FIELD IN THE SAME SCOPE.** That same function read the customer claim as `app_metadata?.customer_id || user_metadata?.customer_id` - three lines below its own SEC-TENANT-003 comment explaining that the session holder writes `user_metadata` through `supabase.auth.updateUser` and it therefore cannot decide anything on a service-role client. The tenant half had been fixed and the customer field sat beside it untouched. Impact checked rather than assumed, and it changes the write-up: **nothing in this repo sets `customer_id` on any user in either bag**, so no portal customers exist yet and this closed a door before the feature that opens it ships. For whoever finishes portal provisioning: set `app_metadata.customer_id` - `user_metadata` is ignored deliberately now, and a user provisioned into the wrong bag is silently treated as staff.

A WORKLIST ENTRY THAT NAMES A CONTROL IS A SEARCH TERM, NOT JUST A NOTE (SEC-EDGE-001). `ai-gpt5` sat on needs-gate from the day that story opened, correctly reasoned: zero writes, but every call costs money at OpenAI and any tenant member can make one, so a level check would look like a fix and change nothing. It wanted a rate limit. **`ai-employee` already had one** - it limits exactly its Claude-hitting POSTs per TENANT with `PRESETS.aiGenerationPerTenant` while its cheap reads pay nothing - and the triage had the two in different buckets with nobody connecting them. Grep for the control before building it. Applied to `ai-gpt5` the seam is cleaner than the original: every paid call is a POST past the existing method check, and `GET /configs` is a static list on the free side. **Per TENANT, not per user** - the bill is the tenant's and a per-user bucket is defeated by any tenant with several accounts - answering 429 with `retryAfterSeconds` and rethrowing anything that is not a `RateLimitError`. `server/tests/unit/ai-spend-rate-limit.test.ts` locks both sides so the pattern cannot drift apart again.

NAMING A MEMBER IS NAMING A COUNT WITH EXTRA STEPS, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (SEC-EDGE-001). One round widened `edge-rbac-catalogue-writes` away from `toEqual(['ai-gpt5'])`, because honestly filing a SECOND needs-gate entry had broken it - and left `toContain('ai-gpt5')` behind as a hedge. That broke the very next round when the entry was RESOLVED. The properties are the floor (`length > 0`, so the list cannot empty itself by reclassification) and the per-entry reason check; the membership pin was neither. **And the same trap reaches mutation testing**: a mutant replacing `PRESETS.aiGenerationPerTenant` appeared to survive because the first occurrence of that string was inside the JSDoc explaining the change, not the call site. Anchor a mutation on the code, exactly as an absence assertion must strip comments first.

GATE THE SCOPE WHEN A BRANCH SERVES BOTH "MINE" AND "EVERYONE'S" (SEC-EDGE-001). `outreach`'s `PUT /business-context` takes `scope: 'tenant' | 'user'`. The user row is a rep tuning how their own outreach reads; the TENANT row is written with `user_id = null` and is the company-wide default every generated prospect email falls back to, so any member could rewrite how the company describes itself. Gating the branch would have removed the personal override, which is the thing reps use - the check is `if (parsed.scope !== 'user')`, placed before both the update and the insert. **The sibling was CHECKED rather than assumed**: `/specializations` is a PUT sitting next to it in the router and looks identical from that line, but `replaceMine` already filters on `ctx.userId`. One router line is not enough to classify a handler.

A FUNCTION THAT STORES SECRETS IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY ONE THAT EXPOSES THEM (SEC-EDGE-001). `manufacturer_connections` holds `api_key`, `api_secret`, `client_secret`, `access_token`, `refresh_token` and `webhook_secret`, and `signatures`' integration credentials are DocuSign/Adobe keys - both looked like the round's big leak from the column list alone, and both already run every read through a redactor (`_credentials.ts` in each case, with envelope encryption on the signatures side). Check the read path before writing up a credential exposure. **The gates that remained were different sizes**: signatures' four credential writes got SUPERVISOR (mirroring `/esignature-integration` minLevel 3), while `manufacturer-orders` is filed **needs-gate and deliberately NOT gated** - placing an order is spending money, but its only caller sits on `/purchase-orders`, which has NO minLevel, so mirroring navigation constrains nobody and gating higher breaks the dialog including its own add-a-manufacturer flow. The control it wants is a spend limit or an approval on the order, not a role. Third entry on that worklist now, alongside `ai-gpt5` and `contract-tiered-rates`, and they share a shape: **when the honest gate level is the page's own and the page is open, a role is the wrong instrument and saying so beats installing one.**

DERIVE WHICH FUNCTIONS WRITE BEFORE PICKING ONE TO EXAMINE (SEC-EDGE-001). Four rounds running recorded a verdict reasoned from a name being overturned, so the next pass counted `.insert/.update/.upsert/.delete` across the remaining `unexamined` functions and read the top of that list instead. A function with zero writes is usually open-by-design; the ones with many are where the decisions are. It found `webhooks` and `pipeline-config`. **`pipeline-config` is the worked example for the branch gate and worth keeping as one**: eight template and stage writes change the deals board for the WHOLE TENANT - a stage rename, a `default_probability` change (which COP-M07 made the forecast weighting, so it moves everyone's forecast too), an `include_in_forecast` flip, a delete - while `/deals/:id/move` and `/deals/:id/transition`, IN THE SAME FUNCTION, are what a rep does all day on that board. The two-line `req.method !== 'GET'` check CLAUDE.md offers as the cheap option would have silently broken drag-and-drop for every rep in the product. Gated at MANAGER to mirror the page's minLevel 4; reads stay open because a rep has to fetch the stages to render the columns. **`webhooks`**: an outbound webhook sends this tenant's data to a URL the caller chooses, so CREATE is an exfiltration primitive and `regenerate-secret` breaks whatever live integration verifies signatures; all five writes now need SUPERVISOR, mirroring the LOWER of the two pages that reach it (`/integration-hub` 3, `/system-integrations` 4) because gating at 4 breaks the page at 3. Its reads were already safe by construction - every one selects an explicit column list omitting `secret` via `_shared/webhook-view.ts` - which is what made leaving them open the right call rather than a concession.

PINNING MEMBERSHIP IS PINNING A COUNT WEARING A LIST'S CLOTHES (SEC-EDGE-001). `edge-rbac-catalogue-writes.test.ts` asserted the needs-gate worklist was exactly `['ai-gpt5']`, so a round that EXAMINED a function and honestly filed it as needs-gate broke the test - it failed because the list grew CORRECTLY, which is the opposite of what it guards. Widened to the property: every needs-gate entry must say why a level check is not the answer, with a floor so emptying the list by reclassification still fails. Fourth round running that a weak assertion shape survived or misfired: a grep matching a substring of a broken name, an assertion anchored to a file rather than a returned object, a count standing in for a per-site check, and now membership standing in for quality. **The same round produced one of my own**: each gated write branch was checked by slicing 260 characters from its header, so the mutant that ungated `bulk-assign` - a rep taking a colleague's whole book in one call - survived, because the window ran past that branch into the next one, which has its own gate. Bound a per-branch assertion to the branch (stop at the next `if (req.method`), never to a fixed window.

TWO MORE BRANCH GATES, AND THE VERDICTS MATTER AS MUCH AS THE GATES (SEC-EDGE-001). `sales-rep-assignments`' three POSTs move an account, its pipeline and whatever commission follows it between reps; gated at SUPERVISOR to mirror the page's `minLevel: 3`, reads left open because they are a territory map a rep needs. `churn-risk`'s `PUT /settings` is tenant-wide - the weights and thresholds that decide which customers the WHOLE COMPANY sees as at risk - so any member could have raised the threshold until the board was empty, and **an empty at-risk board is indistinguishable from a healthy book**; gated at MANAGER, reads open. Three verdicts from the same pass are worth more than the gates. `users` reads as a user directory and IS an assignee picker (one GET, active users, id/email/name/roleId, on pages that are minLevel 1) - gating it would break task assignment for every rep. `sales-forecasts` is open-by-design only because NOTHING WRITES `sales_forecasts` (PA-022), so it becomes needs-gate the day a create path ships. And `contract-tiered-rates` is filed needs-gate and deliberately NOT gated: it sets what a customer is charged per page, but its page is `minLevel: 2`, so mirroring navigation constrains nobody while gating higher locks out the billing staff it exists for - the control it wants is an approval on a rate change, which is the `ai-gpt5` precedent. One method note: `meetings` carried the thin reason "the caller's own calendar" and `grep user.id` on its `index.ts` returns ZERO, which reads as unbacked - the filtering is in `handlers/connections.ts` and `handlers/events.ts`, one directory away. **Check the handlers before overturning a verdict on a delegating dispatcher.**

A NAV GATE HIDES A MENU ITEM AND PROTECTS NOTHING (COP-I06). `/sales-pipeline-forecasting` is `minLevel: 3` in `navigation-permissions.ts`, which is what made "a rep sees the whole team's commit" look contained - but `supabase/functions/pipeline-forecast` enforced NO role check at all and filtered on `tenant_id` alone, so any authenticated member of the tenant could request `/categories` directly and read every rep's forecast number beside their name. `docs/edge-rbac-baseline.json` had it in `openToAllRoles` the whole time; that list is the authority on this, not the nav table. **The fix narrows ROWS rather than refusing the request**, which is the right shape whenever the caller legitimately has some of the data: a rep has a forecast and should see it. `_shared/scope.ts` (WF-R-04) is the helper, and its degradation rule is load-bearing - a tier the org structure cannot answer falls back to the NARROWER one, because guessing wide is a leak while guessing narrow is a manager seeing less than they should. **Scope each table on the column it actually has**: `quotes` carries only `created_by`, `proposals` adds `assigned_to`, `forecast_snapshots` has `owner_id` (who it was taken FOR) beside `captured_by` (who took it) - scoping a table on a column it lacks filters nothing and reads as protected. And **a narrowed total that does not say it was narrowed is a wrong number, not a safe one**: every branch returns the tier, whether it covers the whole tenant, and what it degraded from, plus a caveat naming the one thing that stays unscoped (`sales_goals` has no owner column, so a scoped rep's pipeline is measured against a COMPANY target and `remaining.progressPercent` would otherwise read as a personal shortfall).

A TOTAL IS NOT A PROPERTY (COP-I06). The first version of that story's test counted `applyUserScope(` calls and required at least as many as there were deal reads. The mutant that unscoped the categories query - the exact defect the story exists to fix - SURVIVED, because the other five calls kept the count up. Walk the individual sites and check each one; add a floor so a walk that matches nothing cannot pass in silence. Third round running that a weak assertion shape survived a mutant: a grep matched a substring of a broken name, an assertion anchored to a file rather than a returned object, and now a count standing in for a per-site check.

A SWEEP ON A SCHEDULE MUST PAGE, NOT CAP - AND THE README THAT LISTS THE SCHEDULES DRIFTS TOO (QUALITY-002). `POST /deal-desk/check-sla` shipped saying its pg_cron entry was "Phase 6 US-026"; it was never built, `drizzle/cron/` had no deal-desk file, and no client calls the endpoint, so `sla_breached` flipped only on a manual curl. **That column feeds the deal desk's own compliance analytics, so every unmarked breach made the SLA figures wrong in the flattering direction** - COP-B04's shape with a reporting consequence. Now on `drizzle/cron/deal-desk-sla.sql`, hourly at :35 rather than nightly like every other HTTP job here, because an SLA deadline is a promise measured in hours and a nightly sweep stamps `escalated_at` up to 24 hours late. Two things the port had to FIX rather than copy: the inline version's flat `.limit(500)` is COP-I01's silent truncation on a schedule, where it is worse than on a screen because **each run re-finds the same first 500 and the backlog never clears** (page until a short page comes back - no offset needed, since the update removes each row from the next query's result set, which also makes the job self-healing after a missed hour); and the write keeps its `tenant_id` filter beside the id list (SEC-TENANT-005), which is easy to drop when moving a query into a function that already took `tenantId` as an argument. Separately: `drizzle/cron/README.md` said "Total: 16 jobs" while its table listed 19, stale in the one document whose job is to answer what runs on a schedule - the same failure its own note records about `booking-reminders`. It is asserted now, not maintained: `server/tests/unit/deal-desk-sla-schedule.test.ts` checks the jobs actually `cron.schedule`d across the directory, the README rows and the printed total are the same set and the same size, in both directions.

A RETENTION POLICY ON A TABLE NOTHING WRITES READS LIKE A CONTROL THAT RUNS (QUALITY-002). `server/services/payment-audit-service.ts` is the only writer of `payment_audit_trail` and `payment_method_changes` and NOTHING IMPORTS IT, so both tables have never held a row and every payment went unlogged by it - while `data-retention-service.ts` declares `payment_audit_trail: 2555` days, "7 years (PCI requirement)". Two files, each true, and nobody had put them together. It is KEPT rather than deleted (the requirement is real and this is its only implementation) with a header stating the gap and `server/tests/unit/payment-audit-not-wired.test.ts` designed to FAIL the day someone wires it - the AUDIT-034 treatment. **Its type errors were a latent defect, and the shape recurs with any pinned SDK**: `invoice.subscription` was removed in Stripe API version 2025-03-31.basil and moved to `invoice.parent.subscription_details.subscription`, while this codebase pins `2024-11-20.acacia`, so reading the flat field is a type error AND runtime-correct - precisely where `as string` looks obvious and is wrong, because a cast keeps compiling and starts writing NULL into a compliance record the day the pin moves, indistinguishable from a real one-off payment. Read both shapes. (`Stripe.StripeError` is `Stripe.errors.StripeError` in v18.)

`import.meta.main` IS A DENO API AND IS ALWAYS UNDEFINED IN NODE (QUALITY-002). `server/seed-sales-metrics.ts` ended with `if (import.meta.main) { seedSalesMetrics()... }`, so `tsx server/seed-sales-metrics.ts` exited having seeded nothing - a script that does nothing and reports nothing. Worth grepping for whenever a Node entry point appears inert. Deleted along with `approval-workflow-service.ts` (1148 lines, zero importers, fully covered by `supabase/functions/deal-desk`). **What disappeared is recorded and is a story**: the edge function sends no email, and its own `/check-sla` header says notification is "Phase 3+" and the pg_cron entry is "Phase 6 US-026" - `drizzle/cron/` has no deal-desk file, so an approval SLA breach is marked only when somebody POSTs that endpoint by hand, and no client calls it. COP-B04's shape, one story short of done.

A GREP ASSERTION CAN SURVIVE A MUTANT BY MATCHING A SUBSTRING OF THE BROKEN NAME (QUALITY-002). A test checked an accessor's body contained `subscription_details`; renaming the property to `subscription_detailsX` left that intact and the test green. Reading source proves the text is there, only CALLING it proves the value comes out - so export the pure accessor and test it with real inputs wherever the property is the point. Same week, the same lesson in a different costume: an assertion anchored to a FILE rather than to the returned OBJECT passed while a response key was renamed.

AN UNPROXIED PREFIX WITH AN EDGE FUNCTION BESIDE IT IS A PARITY QUESTION, AND ONE MISSING BRANCH CAN TAKE A WHOLE PAGE DOWN (QUALITY-002). `/api/gdpr` is not in `crmProxies`, so dev runs `routes-gdpr-core.ts` and production runs `supabase/functions/gdpr`. `GdprComplianceDashboard.tsx` issues four queries through ONE `QueryStates` wrapper, and two of them - `GET /consent/stats` and `GET /data-export/requests` - had no edge branch at all, so the whole dashboard rendered "Could not load compliance data" on the deployed host while working on every developer machine. **A single-wrapper page fails whole, not per card**, which makes one unported endpoint worth as much as four. Both are ported (head-count for the totals, one two-column fetch for the GROUP BY tallies PostgREST cannot do, and export rows CAMELISED because the page reads `exp.exportNumber` off each row). The third defect there is PA-040's key-name shape with a twist worth recognising: the Active DPAs card reads `expiringIn30Days` under a label saying "expiring in 30 days" and the endpoint sent `expiringIn` over a NINETY-day window, so the key the page wanted was never sent AND the number sent answered a different question - **both sides individually defensible, neither compared to the other**. Also deleted from that page: `const complianceScore = 85` rendered as 85% with a progress bar and "Based on consent, DPA, and data handling metrics" underneath. `check:no-static-posture` scans that directory and missed it because the guard matches a bare value as a JSX TEXT NODE, and this one goes through a variable - `{complianceScore}%` is an expression, so **a fabricated figure escapes that guard by being assigned to a name first**.

DELETING A FILE A TEST NAMES IS AN INVITATION TO WIDEN THE TEST (QUALITY-002). `month-arithmetic-closed.test.ts` asserted the `setMonth(x.getMonth() + n)` idiom was absent from twelve NAMED files, one of which was an orphaned seeder being retired. It walks five roots now, 1,000+ files, with a corpus-size floor so a walk that matches nothing cannot pass in silence, and two exclusions by RULE rather than by name: `server/tests/` (those tests demonstrate the old idiom, so asserting its absence over its own proof reports the explanation as the defect) and `date-months.ts` (the replacement, which steps a cursor pinned to day 1 where setMonth cannot overflow). Same move as the `sql-injection-prevention.test.ts` widening. Two process notes from the same round: **a surviving mutant meant the assertion matched the wrong thing** - renaming a response key left the test green because a local `const` of the same name still carried the string, so anchor a shape assertion to the returned OBJECT, not to the file; and **`git checkout <file>` to undo a mutation destroys unstaged work in that file**, which happened twice in one session - back mutations up with `cp` and restore with `cp`.

A BACKEND THAT TYPES ITS POSTURE IN IS INVISIBLE TO ALL THREE FABRICATION GUARDS (QUALITY-002). `GET /cross-module/status` answered `healthy: true`, five modules each `'connected'`, `pendingEvents: 0`, `processedToday: 0` and a `lastSync` of `new Date()`, with no query behind any of it - and `CrossModuleIntegration.tsx`, rendered by the live ServiceHub, turned that into a **"100% Healthy" badge over a full progress bar** plus a last-sync time that always read as a moment ago. `check:no-static-posture` scans `client/src/pages` and this is a component; `check:fabricated` wants a `|| literal` and the page had no fallback BECAUSE its endpoint answered; `check:no-random-metrics` wants `Math.random()`. This shape is harder to spot than the page-level one: the page calls an endpoint, gets a 200, and renders what it is told. Two rules from the fix. The claim could not be true in the sense it was read - these "modules" are tables in one database, not services that can be disconnected, so there was no link whose health it could describe; when an endpoint cannot be wrong, check whether it can be right. And the counters are null rather than 0, because **a 0 reads as "no backlog", which is a measurement**, while no cross-module event table exists in any schema; all three gaps are named in an `unbacked` array. `POST /log-event` returned 200 with `eventId: EVT-<Date.now()>` beside a comment saying it "could store in a dedicated events table if needed" - an identifier for something it discarded - and answers 501 now.

A MOUNT TABLE IS WHERE A ROUTER GOES TO BE FORGOTTEN (QUALITY-002). `check:shadowed-express` matched mount tuples with a closing bracket demanded straight after the module string, so `routes-registry.ts`'s `lazyModules` entries - which carry a third element, the label - never matched, and all five of those routers were invisible to the gate. Two sat on proxied prefixes: 26 handlers dead on arrival. The pattern takes an optional trailing element now. Both were deleted and the reasons differ, which is the part worth copying: `routes-cross-module.ts` duplicated its edge function endpoint for endpoint with a live caller that never noticed, while `routes-territory-management.ts` and its service duplicated `supabase/functions/lead-assignment` over the same four tables with NO client calling `/api/territories` on either host - COP-B09 had rebuilt that page against `/api/sales-territories` and said so. Note both `territories` and `lead-assignment` are in `docs/unreferenced-edge-fns-baseline.json`, so lead routing now has one implementation and no reachable surface.

A `useState` INITIALIZER IS NOT AN EFFECT, AND ONE OF THE TWO INSTANCES WAS CORRECT BY ACCIDENT (QUALITY-002). `WhiteLabelDashboard.tsx` copied the saved configuration into its form with `useState(() => { if (config) setFormData(...) })`. That initializer runs ONCE, during the first render, before the query resolves, so `config` was always undefined and the `if` never fired: the form kept its blank defaults whatever the tenant had saved, and Save Changes then PUT those blanks plus the three placeholder colours over the real branding, because the edge function passes an empty string straight through to the column. Opening a routed page and clicking one button wiped company name, tagline, logo, custom domain, welcome copy and both support contacts, and it looked like a first-time setup screen the whole time. `npm run check:usestate-as-effect` is a HARD GATE AT ZERO over all seven client trees, and its rule is deliberately not about timing - the other instance (`SalesRepAssignments`' zip-centroid load) genuinely wanted mount timing and got it. The rule is that a `useState` whose VALUE GOES NOWHERE is never what was meant; lazy init (`const [x] = useState(() => ...)`) is consumed and is fine. Two things the fix encodes wherever a form mirrors a query: hydrate on the FIRST row to arrive behind a ref, since a plain `useEffect([data])` re-runs on every refetch and discards what the user has typed; and MERGE a stored jsonb settings blob over the defaults, or the keys it omits render as uncontrolled inputs. The Express half of this pair, `server/services/white-label-service.ts`, is deleted - its only reference in the tree was a comment in the live Deno copy saying "KEEP IN SYNC with" it, which is an instruction to maintain a file nothing runs. Check what the other side of a sync note actually covers before believing either half is load-bearing.

A BARE `fetch('/api/...')` IS BROKEN IN PRODUCTION AND FINE IN DEV, AND FIXING THE URL IS THE SMALL HALF (PROD-013). A relative fetch skips `getApiUrl`, so in production it resolves against whatever origin serves the static bundle rather than the API, and `credentials: 'include'` sends cookies where an edge function wants a Bearer JWT. `check:raw-fetch` took this from 37 sites to 9, **all of them now on unreachable files - no reachable page makes one**. Four rules came out of that sweep and each cost a real defect to learn. **CHECK THE PROD-SIDE BRANCH AND THE RESPONSE SHAPE BEFORE CONVERTING**, because correcting a URL turns "no data" into "wrong data" when the shapes disagree: the subscriptions edge fn answered `{ data: plans }` where the hook reads `{ plans, features }`, and the rbac fn answered bare arrays where the page reads `rolesData.roles` and `permissionsData.totalCount`. Worse, rbac's list read `roles`, the GLOBAL catalogue, while the status tile beside it counts `enhanced_roles` - a URL fix alone would have produced a tile counting one table above a list drawn from another. **MULTIPART NEEDS `apiFormRequest`**: `apiRequest` JSON-stringifies its body, so converting a FormData post to it sends the string `"[object FormData]"` and loses the file, while typechecking cleanly. **`apiRequest` RETURNS PARSED JSON, NOT A RESPONSE** - a leftover `if (!res.ok)` after the swap is permanently false and a leftover `res.json()` is a TypeError. And **A MENTION IS NOT A CALL**, which is the same shape `check:no-static-posture` was caught on: a file can import `useQuery` and never use it. WHAT REMAINS is filed as PROD-STRIPE-001 at priority 1: seven Stripe paths - checkout, checkout/addon, checkout/session/:id, portal, setup-intent, preview-upgrade, stripe/config - exist on Express alone, so nobody can subscribe or manage payment in production. They 404 visibly now rather than dissolving into the static origin, which is the better failure but not a fix.

A CALENDAR DATE IS NOT AN INSTANT, AND THE RULE IS NOW WRITTEN DOWN (DATE-LOCAL-002). Several columns here are `timestamp` in the schema but hold a CALENDAR DATE - `meter_readings.reading_date`, `time_entries.entry_date`, `tasks.due_date`, `invoices.invoice_date` and `paid_date`, journal entry dates, contract start and end. DATE-LOCAL-001 made the client write them as a local `yyyy-MM-dd`, so Postgres stores MIDNIGHT. A bound built from `new Date()` carries a time of day, so the comparison lands half a day off and the window is wrong by exactly one day, silently, in whichever direction the operator points. **The rule: a range filter on a calendar-date column is snapped to a day boundary - `startOfUtcDay` for a lower bound, `startOfNextUtcDay` with a strict `.lt()` for an upper one, both from `supabase/functions/_shared/date-months.ts`. An instant column (`created_at`, `resolved_at`, `triggered_at`) is compared to an instant and needs none of this.** Prefer the exclusive next-day bound over an inclusive `23:59:59.999`, which is a real timestamp a row can exceed. WHOSE MIDNIGHT: `tenants` HAS NO TIMEZONE COLUMN - checked, not assumed, and `user_settings.timezone` is per-user and unavailable to a cron - so UTC is not a shortcut here, it is the only defensible answer, and it happens to be exact because the stored values are UTC midnight too. Three live instances this closed, each a different consequence: the customer portal's usage analytics dropped every reading dated on the first day of its window AND, because the same boundary splits this period from the previous one, counted those readings in the WRONG HALF of the comparison the page is built on; `tasks` stats counted everything due TODAY as overdue from 00:00, while the "due today" tile beside it counted the same rows again, so the two figures overlapped; and a 30-day hours total in teams analytics was 29 days. `npm run check:calendar-date-bounds` is a shrink-only ratchet at 36 - a ratchet and not a gate because the column list is a NAMING CONVENTION, not a type: everything is `timestamp`, so nothing distinguishes `reading_date` from `sent_date` except reading the feature.

NOTHING TYPECHECKS THE EDGE TREE, SO A MISSING IMPORT IS A RUNTIME 500 (MONEY-FORMAT-002's other half). Deno files are outside the tsc project: an undefined identifier is not a compile error, it is a ReferenceError the first time the code path runs. PERF-ROWCAP-002 converted 33 call sites to `fetchAllRows` and left EIGHT without the import - daily-briefing, qbr, commission, contract-renewal, churn-risk, payment-processing, predictive-failure, renewal-autoquote. Two unit tests caught two; the other six would have shipped. `npm run check:shared-helper-imports` is the gate now, hard at zero: every `_shared` export that is CALLED must be imported, with namespace calls (`google.withAutoRefresh`), string literals, local declarations and `await import` destructuring all excluded by rule. Two things it cost to get right and both are general. STRIP LINE COMMENTS BEFORE BLOCK COMMENTS: `reports/handlers/sales.ts` opens `// Sales-persona reports - /reports/sales/*`, and a block-comment pass run first reads that trailing `/*` as an opener and blanks the next forty lines, imports included - 77 findings, nearly all of it that. And keep the `https://` lookbehind while doing it, which is the mirror-image bug `check:seo-assets` already carries. Second: a stub that mocks a PostgREST builder needs `.range()` the moment a caller is converted to `fetchAllRows`, or the test fails with `build(...).range is not a function` rather than anything about paging.

A FUNCTION CAN RETURN VARIABLES IT NEVER DECLARED, AND NOTHING HERE WILL TELL YOU (COP-B04, round 60). `opportunity-radar`'s `runScan` resolved its settings, honoured the kill switch, computed `thresholds` and `now`, then returned `{ detected: drafts.length, created: inserted, machinesScanned: equipment.length }` - **three identifiers never declared in the function**. It fetched nothing and never called `detectPlays`, which sat imported and unused. Deno is outside the tsc project, so that is a ReferenceError the first time the path runs, not a compile error. Both entry points hit it, and the nightly sweep's own per-tenant catch - written deliberately so one tenant's failure does not abort the rest - **recorded the failure and stepped over it**, so an all-failing sweep reads as a quiet night unless somebody opens the response body. The radar had detected nothing since it shipped while looking finished from every angle: the page exists, the cron file exists, the pure detector exists with forty passing tests, the edge function is 500 lines. **The tell was a variable assigned and never read** (`thresholds`), which is the one thing lint would have caught had the file been in its scope. When a story's deliverables all exist, check that something CALLS them - AUDIT-024's rule one level in, at function-body granularity.

`npm run check:edge-undef` CLOSES THE GAP COP-B04 FELL THROUGH, AND ITS FIRST RUN FOUND SIX MORE (round 61). `eslint.config.js` ignores `supabase/functions/**` with the comment "checked by `deno check` / check:edge-paths, not ESLint". **Neither does the job**: there is no `deno.json` anywhere and no deno step in CI, and `check:edge-paths` only checks path normalization. So across 257 edge functions nothing had ever reported an identifier that does not exist, and Deno is outside the tsc project, so that is a ReferenceError the first time the path runs. What one run turned up, every one live:

- `webhooks` and `sales-rep-assignments` - the SEC-EDGE-001 gate helpers were
  declared inside an `if (!tenantId) { return ... }` block that was never
  closed, so eight write branches called helpers that do not exist in their
  scope. **Both were my own regressions from earlier in this session**, and
  every guard already in the repo passed on them.
- `printer-monitoring` - `tenantId` used 26 lines ABOVE its own `const`, in
  another branch: every agent metrics submission threw "Cannot access
  'tenantId' before initialization".
- `calendar-oauth` - `admin` borrowed from a branch that returns first.
- `_shared/manufacturer-adapters` - `Buffer.from(...)` and
  `require('crypto')`, both Node globals that **do not exist in Deno**, so the
  Basic-auth header and the HMAC signature threw before a request was sent.
  Replaced with `btoa` and Web Crypto (async, so the three call sites await).

**AN UNCLOSED `if (!tenantId)` BLOCK IS THE SHAPE TO RECOGNISE**: the early return means everything after it inside the block is dead, so the declarations look present, prettier formats them happily, and the failure only appears when a caller reaches a branch that uses them. Grep is no help - the identifier IS in the file.

TWO RULES, TWO POSTURES, and the split is what keeps the gate running. `no-undef` is a HARD GATE at zero: every finding is a guaranteed runtime error and there is no correct version of one. `no-unused-vars` is a SHRINK-ONLY ratchet at 73 (`docs/edge-unused-vars-baseline.json`), because most of its findings are harmless while a few are the COP-B04 tell - **a local computed and never read is usually a body that was removed or never written**. Mixing them would mean tolerating real ReferenceErrors or blocking on cosmetics, and a gate that blocks on cosmetics stops being run. `eslint.edge.config.js` declares Deno's globals explicitly rather than importing a globals package, so a name that is not on the list is one nothing here should be calling; the TS lib TYPE names (`CryptoKey`, `RequestInit`, `BlobPart`) are on it because `no-undef` cannot tell a type position from a value one.

THE SCAN THAT REPLACED IT ENCODES FIVE RULES THIS REPO ALREADY PAID FOR, and they generalise to any sweep: page every read with `fetchAllRows` rather than capping (a flat `.limit()` on a schedule re-finds the same first page every night and the tail is never looked at); derive meter volume from the LIFETIME COUNTERS, never `black_copies`/`color_copies`, which default to 0 so an unfinished import is indistinguishable from a month of no printing (COP-B05); a counter reading LOWER than it did yields no rate rather than a negative one, because that is a reset or a swapped machine; idempotency is the UNIQUE INDEX with `ignoreDuplicates`, not a scan-time lookup, because two overlapping sweeps both read "not present" and both insert - and the count reported as created must be what the database actually wrote, or `detected - created` stops meaning "already known"; and an insert error THROWS, because a sweep that swallows it reports rows it did not store.

TWO ASSERTION TRAPS FIRED AGAIN IN THE SAME HOUR, both already in this file. An absence check for `black_copies` matched the comment explaining why that column is NOT used - **fourth occurrence**; strip comments on both sides of any absence assertion, always. And `expect(src).toContain('fetchAllRows')` was satisfied by any one of five calls, so un-paging just the equipment read survived the mutant - **third occurrence** of a presence check standing in for a per-site one. The fix in both cases is the same shape: bind the assertion to the site, not the file.

THE ONE CURRENCY FORMATTER NOW HAS THREE ENTRY POINTS, AND THE THIRD IS NOT A DEFECT (MONEY-FORMAT-002). 53 local copies became 1. `formatCurrency` in `client/src/lib/utils.ts` keeps cents, coerces the string a Drizzle decimal arrives as from Express, and renders an em dash for a missing value because a missing price is not a free one. `formatCurrencyWhole` is that with cents off, for an annual total or a dashboard aggregate. `formatCurrencyCompact` is $1.2M / $45K, for an axis label or a leaderboard cell - TEN components had written their own because the shared formatter had no compact mode, and converting those to full currency would have blown out every table column, so check what a copy DOES before replacing it. Both variants are named exports rather than an options bag because these formatters are usually passed BY REFERENCE - to a recharts `tickFormatter`, to `metricOrDash` - where there is no argument list to add an option to; the same fact is why `tickFormatter={formatCurrency}` does not typecheck (recharts passes an index as the second argument) and needs a wrapping arrow. `export-utils` keeps its own entry point for one reason: a missing value in a CSV cell is EMPTY, not an em dash and certainly not the `$0.00` it used to write, which is a claim that the row is free and survives being summed.

SPREADING A REQUEST BODY INTO POSTGREST IS TWO DEFECTS IN ONE LINE (COP-M01). `.update({ ...body, updated_at })` lets the BODY decide every column name, and it lets the body name columns the caller should never set. `PUT /leads/:id` was exactly this: LeadDetail sends 30 camelCase fields, PostgREST wants columns, so every save was a PGRST204 surfaced as "Failed to update lead" - **in production only**, because `/api/leads` is not proxied and dev goes through `storage.updateLead` -> Drizzle, which maps field names to columns. Editing a lead worked on every developer machine and on no deployed one. The second half is the security one: `.eq('tenant_id', tenantId)` decides WHICH row a write lands on, not what is written INTO it, so a body carrying `tenant_id` moved the record to another tenant, and `id`, `created_by`, `converted_by` and `deactivated_by` were writable the same way. SEC-TENANT-005 is the FILTER half of tenant-scoped writes; this is the payload half. The fix shape is `supabase/functions/_shared/business-record-write.ts`: an explicit column whitelist locked against drizzle's `getTableColumns` by a test (a hand-copied list is a phantom column waiting for a schema change), both spellings accepted, unwritable columns refused, and whatever it drops REPORTED on the response as `ignoredFields`/`refusedFields` rather than swallowed - an empty plan answers 400, never a 200 that bumps `updated_at` and reports success. FOUR FIELDS DO NOT CAMELISE TO THEIR COLUMN and would be dropped in silence: `estimatedAmount`/`estimatedDealValue` -> `estimated_deal_value`, `leadSource` -> `source`, `churnReason` -> `deactivation_reason`, `billingPostalCode` -> `billing_zip_code`. `npm run check:raw-body-writes` gates the class at **13** baselined; `_shared/chart-of-accounts-contract.ts` records the same shape being fixed once before with no guard built, which is why it came back.

A TENANT-SCOPED WRITE IS BOUND IN THE PAYLOAD OR IN THE FILTER, AND THEY ARE DIFFERENT RULES (SEC-TENANT-005). `check:insert-tenant-id` now covers all four verbs. INSERT and UPSERT need `tenant_id` in the payload: a NOT NULL column fails the write outright (the first save of accessibility settings 500'd for every user), and a nullable one writes a row no tenant-filtered read can ever see, so the user is told it saved and it is gone. UPDATE and DELETE do not need it in the payload at all - they need it in the FILTER, or the write reaches ANOTHER TENANT'S ROW, which is what SEC-TENANT-001 found in parts-orders. An id is not a substitute: these are uuids, so guessing one is impractical, but they travel in URLs, exports and support tickets, and hard-to-guess is not an authorisation check. THREE THINGS COUNT AS BOUND for an update or delete, and the guard knows all three: a `tenant_id` filter on the chain; a write scoped to the authenticated user (`.eq('user_id', user.id)` cannot cross a tenant, because a user is in one); or a PRECEDING OWNERSHIP READ of the same table filtered by id AND tenant_id that 404s when absent, which is what saved-views does. Filtering the write as well is still better and cheaper than proving the check. The insert/upsert half is a HARD GATE at zero; the filter half is a SHRINK-ONLY ratchet at 30 (`docs/tenant-write-filter-baseline.json`), and those 30 are PRINTED on every run rather than hidden, because each needs its own call - blog-pipeline is an internal job runner, public-booking is deliberately unauthenticated off a booking token, and several fetch the row too far above the write for any fixed lookback. WHAT IT CANNOT RESOLVE IS COUNTED, NOT SKIPPED: 107 payloads built by an expression or by a helper, each printed with its location, so a clean run means "nothing resolvable is wrong" rather than "nothing is wrong". `.insert(payloadVariable)` is by far the commonest shape and the old version matched only an inline literal, so it never saw a single one of them.

Drizzle drops unknown keys, on writes AND inserts (QUALITY-002 batches 26-27): `.set()` and `.values()` both iterate the TABLE's columns and pick each one out of the object you hand them, so a key the table does not have is IGNORED — no error, no warning. `.set({technicianId})` on `service_tickets` reduced to `set updated_at = $1` and the endpoint reported success while assigning nobody (the real column is `assigned_technician_id`). `.values({name: 'Acme'})` on `companies` emitted `default` for `business_name`, which is NOT NULL, so `npm run seed:demo` died at phase 2. tsc catches keys written DIRECTLY in the literal; a `...row` spread turns excess-property checking OFF for everything it carries, which is how ~30 dropped keys sat invisible in the demo seeder. `npm run check:spread-insert-keys` (hard gate at zero) resolves `for (const v of ARR)` spreads and reports keys that are not columns; it cannot see spreads of function args, mapped rows or `...req.body`. The demo seed set is now ONE module, `server/seeds/demo-data.ts`, shared by `npm run seed:demo` and `POST /api/admin/seed/demo` — it used to be two byte-identical 1,300-line copies that carried the same defects and had to be fixed twice.

Signatures consolidation (EDGE-005e): the frontend now calls the consolidated `/api/signatures/{requests,templates,analytics}` shape (was three flat `/api/signature-*` prefixes that 404'd in prod — no such function dirs). The `signatures/` dispatcher strips an OPTIONAL leading `signature-` from the first path segment, so BOTH `requests` and legacy `signature-requests` resolve (mobile/older callers keep working). Why a refactor and NOT the EDGE-005a server.ts plural→singular alias: server.ts STRIPS the matched fn-name segment before calling the handler, but the signatures dispatcher keys off that segment (the sub-resource) — a plain alias would erase the discriminator. `handlers/analytics.ts` derives REAL aggregates from `signature_requests` (totalContractValue=0 — no contract-value column; byDocumentType groups by `provider` — no document_type column). `handlers/templates.ts` returns `[]` — there is NO `signature_templates` table in any schema/migration (only requests/signers/documents exist). `/send` + `/:id/remind` are STUBS (audit log + status/timestamp bump; no provider envelope/email — DocuSign/Adobe/HelloSign wiring is a deferred PRD). GOTCHA: `ESignatureIntegration.tsx` was built against a MOCK camelCase shape (`requestedDate`/`documentName`/`customerName`/`remindersSent`) that does NOT match the real snake_case `signature_requests` columns — its request list/detail won't render correctly against live data; that data-contract rewrite is a separate story.

Line item order (QUOTE-020): the `lineItems` ARRAY ORDER is the source of truth — `buildQuoteData` persists it as `line_number` (index+1) and every read path (edge fn GET, both PDFs, share page) orders by `line_number`; PricingCalculator maps the array as-is. Drag-reorder in `LineItemManager` moves whole parent groups (sublines follow), rebuilds the array as [parent, ...sublines] blocks + orphan sublines at the end, renumbers 1..n, and hands it up via `onReorderItems`; autosave picks it up because the QUOTE-018 effect watches `lineItems`. Drag starts only from the GripVertical handle (rows are full of inputs) and is disabled in the group-by-type view (a local, non-persisted Switch that clusters by PARENT productType with per-group subtotals). Gotcha: sublines reference their parent by PRODUCT id (`parentLineId === parent.productId`), not the row id — any parent/subline matching must use productId.

Alias-target edge fns (EDGE-005f): three frontend prefixes whose URL segment differs from the edge-function DIR name are resolved the EDGE-005a way — a `server.ts` route override (prod) PLUS a `crmProxies` entry (dev), so both land the same handler path. (1) `/api/deployment/{readiness,metrics}` → `deployment-readiness/` fn (override `deployment`→`deployment-readiness`); the fn now returns the exact `ReadinessCheck[]` / `DeploymentMetrics` shapes `DeploymentReadiness.tsx` types (derived from live tenant signals), not the legacy `{ready,checks}` object — returning the WRONG shape is worse than a 404 because the page does `readinessChecks || mockChecks` and would `.filter` a truthy object. (2) `/api/integration-hub/dashboard` → `integrations/` fn `/dashboard` branch (override `integration-hub`→`integrations`); detect it via `normalizePath` BEFORE the `!segment1` GET-list branch or that branch swallows it. (CORRECTED 2026-07-17: the integrations fn HAS since been migrated to normalizePath under PA-024 — it no longer indexes `pathParts[1]` off the raw split. But the pattern it was fixed for is WIDESPREAD and is the single biggest known prod-only defect class in the edge tree — see "Edge path normalization" below.) (3) `/api/public/calculator/*` is the UNAUTHENTICATED marketing calculator → new `public-calculator/` fn (override fires only when `functionName==='public' && subPath[0]==='calculator'`, proxy uses `{fn:'public-calculator',pathPrefix:'/calculator'}` to re-add the segment the mount strips). Auth-bypass reality: `config.toml verify_jwt=false` is for the NATIVE Supabase runtime only — the Coolify `server.ts` dispatcher ignores config.toml and enforces auth PER-HANDLER, so "public" just means the handler never calls `auth.getUser` (same as the `signup` fn). Its pure calc engine is duplicated at `supabase/functions/_shared/print-cost-calculator.ts` (near-verbatim copy of `server/services/print-cost-calculator-service.ts` — keep in sync). Email nurture rows are recorded (`email_sequence_tracking` status='pending') but the provider SEND is stubbed (no email infra in the edge fn). GOTCHA: the `deno check` getUser(jwt) `string|null` vs `string|undefined` TS2345 is a pre-existing pattern across ~40 edge fns (`createSupabaseClient` uses `req.headers.get('Authorization')!`); fix the files you touch by extracting jwt as `... : undefined` not `: null`. `deno lint`'s `no-explicit-any` is unenforced here (no `deno.json` anywhere, not in CI) and the whole edge codebase uses `any` on untyped supabase rows — match that style. The QUALITY-002 typecheck baseline drifts UP from main merges (PR #184 pushed clean main to 3910 vs a 3840 baseline); reconcile it when a story's own tree adds 0 tsc errors, documenting the upstream cause — don't try to "fix" 70 unrelated pre-existing errors.

Platform-admin CRM edge fns (EDGE-004): `/api/platform-{crm,deals,activities,analytics,cs}` are the root-admin platform CRM pages. Only `platform-deals` + `platform-crm` had edge fns before; `platform-activities`/`platform-analytics`/`platform-cs` were Express-only and 404'd in prod. NEW fns mirror the siblings: gate on root admin (`role level >= 7 OR roles.can_access_all_tenants`) via the service-role client, `normalizePath(pathname, '<fn>')`, query the canonical `platform_*` tables in `shared/platform-crm-schema.ts` (NOT `shared/schema.ts`). DECISION RULE for these: dir name == URL prefix segment, so a PLAIN `crmProxies` entry is enough — NO `server.ts` override needed (unlike EDGE-005a/005f where the dir name differed). Two shape rules: (1) COMPUTED analytics endpoints (revenue-metrics/conversion-metrics/pipeline-metrics/performance-metrics/growth-trends/health-trends) must return the EXACT camelCase keys the page reads (`revenueData`,`funnelData`,`distributionData`,`sourceData`,`metrics.{mrr,arr,...}`) — the pages fall back to MOCK data with `|| [...]` so a wrong shape silently shows fake numbers; (2) list endpoints return raw snake_case rows like the `platform-crm` sibling. GOTCHA — the legacy Express handlers referenced PHANTOM columns that don't exist in the schema and would 500 at runtime: `platform_activity_reports.report_date`/`rep_id`/`rep_name`/`calls_logged`/`emails_sent`/`leads_generated` (real cols are `period_start`/`user_id`/`total_calls`/`total_emails`/`meetings_held`/`demos_completed`/`new_deals`/`deals_won`/`total_arr_booked`); `platform_business_records.last_mrr_change` (absent → expansion MRR is always 0); `platform_cohort_analysis.period_type`/`customers_at_start`/`revenue_at_start` (real cols are `cohort_period`/`initial_size`/`initial_mrr`/`current_size`/`retention_rate`); `platform_churn_predictions.churn_risk_level`/`estimated_revenue_impact` (real: `churn_risk`/`estimated_arr`). The edge fns adapt to the REAL columns (an improvement, per the schema-audit AC). SEPARATE-STORY DEBT: `PlatformCustomerSuccess.tsx` types `TenantHealth` (healthGrade/companyName/mrr/daysUntilRenewal/csmId) against a MOCK shape that does NOT match the real `platform_health_scores` columns (overall_score/health_status/...) — the endpoints now RESOLVE (200 instead of prod 404) but the page's field-by-field render is a data-contract rewrite out of scope. Deleted the four `routes-platform-{business-records,activities,analytics,customer-success}.ts` (kept `routes-platform-deals.ts`); deleting the phantom-col files DROPPED the tsc ratchet 3910→3795 (−115) — tighten the baseline. `requireRootAdmin` is exported from `routes-root-admin.ts` (kept) and was the only cross-import.

Plural-prefix→singular-fn alias (EDGE-005a): `/api/accounts-{payable,receivable}` (plural, flat) → `account-{payable,receivable}/` fn dirs (singular). DECISION RULE — when the frontend uses a FLAT prefix (list at `/`, item at `/:id`) and the edge fn dir name only differs by spelling, use a PLAIN proxy entry + server.ts override and make the HANDLER flat (normalizePath, id at `parts[0]`). Do NOT use a `pathPrefix` alias to re-add a nested segment like `/bills`: pathPrefix is dev-only, but prod hits `functions.printyx.net/<prefix>` directly and server.ts strips the function-name segment, so the handler sees a flat path with nothing to inject — pathPrefix would 404 in prod. BIGGER LESSON FROM THIS STORY: these two edge fns were querying ENTIRELY WRONG TABLES (account-payable→`bills`, account-receivable→`invoices`/`payments`) — the canonical tables are `accounts_payable`/`accounts_receivable` (shared/schema.ts), which the legacy Express handler in `server/routes-products-crud.ts` (via `storage.getAccountsPayable/Receivable`) already used. ALWAYS confirm which table the canonical Drizzle schema + legacy Express storage method use before trusting an existing edge fn's `.from('...')`. Edge-fn body inserts must map to REAL columns only (build an explicit camel+snake→column object; drop unknown keys) or PostgREST throws PGRST204; watch NOT NULL columns the form omits — `accounts_receivable.invoice_type` is NOT NULL so default it ('standard'), and both AP/AR `balance_amount` is NOT NULL so default to total−paid. The `check:routes` ratchet baseline is `docs/route-ownership-baseline.json`: after folding a domain, run `node scripts/check-route-ownership.mjs --update-baseline` and review the git diff — it will also absorb unrelated upstream drift (e.g. US-SUPER-008's `deal-desk-copilot` reclassified missingEdge→bothDivergent), so document any non-story domains it moves.

<!-- SELVEDGE:START -->

## Pearson Media — shared context

_Managed from the vault. Edit `14 - Resources/Shared CLAUDE Block.md` in the vault; direct edits between these markers are overwritten once a sync exists. Everything outside them is yours and is never touched._

**The memory vault.** Portfolio-wide memory lives in the **Hermes** vault at `<your-home>\Documents\Hermes` (`C:\Users\dpearson\Documents\Hermes` on this machine; remote: https://github.com/dj-pearson/Hermes). It holds the profile, the map of all ten projects, and cross-project knowledge. Read `VAULT-INDEX.md` there when a task needs context beyond this repo. This repo's own `CLAUDE.md`, `~/.claude` memory, and skills remain authoritative for work inside it — the vault supplements them, never replaces them.

**Name the project.** Pearson Media runs ten projects on a shared stack. Never say "the app," "the repo," or "production" without naming which one. A right answer about the wrong project is a wrong answer.

**The shared stack.** React + TypeScript + Vite, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, self-hosted Supabase, Cloudflare Pages, Coolify on Contabo, Stripe. A problem solved in one repo is usually already solved for this one — check the vault before solving it twice.

**Secrets are references, never values.** Never write a password, key, or token value into a note, summary, commit, or setup doc; name where it's stored instead. Loose credential files exist under your `Documents` folder (`C:\Users\dpearson\Documents` on this machine) — never read one into a document.

**Never delete what Claude Code relies on.** Repo `CLAUDE.md` files, `~/.claude/projects/*/memory/`, `.claude/skills/`, settings. Copy from them freely; removing or stubbing them is Dj's call alone.

**Evidence only.** Verify state from the actual file or command before claiming anything is done or in place. If unsure, say so and go find out.

**Write like a person.** Every model was trained on the same corpus, so the default register is recognisable within a sentence and it lands in commits, PR bodies, docs, UI copy and error strings alike. State the point first, then support it. Have an opinion; asked which of two, name one. Use real names and numbers, not categories. Never label your own significance ("important", "crucial", "worth noting", "notably"); if it matters the reader will see it. Banned outright: _delve, dive into, deep dive, unpack, shed light on, pave the way, usher in, tap into, supercharge, unlock, elevate, empower, streamline, curate, showcase, boast, groundbreaking, cutting-edge, transformative, game-changing, innovative, pivotal, invaluable, meticulous, bespoke, vibrant, multifaceted, holistic, testament, tapestry, synergy, cornerstone, treasure trove, plethora, myriad, moreover, furthermore, additionally._ Banned decoratively but fine literally: _navigate, harness, leverage, robust, comprehensive, landscape, realm, journey_; the test is whether a reader could check the claim. Banned phrases: _"In today's…", "It's important/worth noting", "When it comes to", "At its core", "At the end of the day", "This is where X comes in", "Let's break it down", "plays a crucial role", "cannot be overstated", "underscoring the importance of", "highlighting the need for"_, and the whole chat register (_"Great question!", "Absolutely!", "I'd be happy to", "Let me know if you need anything else", "I hope this helps"_). Banned structures, which imitate insight without carrying any: _"not just X, it's Y"_, _"not only X but Y"_, _"this isn't about X, it's about Y"_, _"No X. No Y. Just Z."_, the rule of three that goes abstract on the third item, the rhetorical question as a transition, and closing with a summary of what was just read. **At most one em dash** per piece of writing, never as the default connector; use commas, parentheses and semicolons. Vary sentence and paragraph length deliberately. Uniform 18-word sentences are the signature that survives every word-level edit. Use contractions. Don't restate the question, don't open with a sweeping scene-setter, don't over-format (no emoji as structure, no header on a three-paragraph answer, no table for two rows). The one allowed exception is a **bold lead-in used as a heading** in a reference document like this one; a _run_ of "**Bold term:** one sentence" bullets standing in for prose is the tell.

**Plain characters only.** Generated text carries Unicode that renders as ordinary punctuation, as ordinary whitespace, or as nothing at all, and it survives review precisely because it looks correct. **Anything a machine parses is ASCII unless the content requires otherwise**: code, config, JSON, YAML, CSV, SQL, regex, env values, filenames, URLs, commit subjects. Straight quotes `'` `"`, hyphen-minus `-`, three dots for an ellipsis, one ordinary space between words. Never emit curly quotes (U+2018/2019/201C/201D), en/em dashes (U+2013/2014), U+2026 ellipsis, U+2212 minus or U+2032 primes into code; a look-alike character in a PowerShell string or a SQL literal is a runtime failure, which is how `backup-databases.ps1` and `ssl-check.ps1` sat unparseable for months. Never emit a no-break space (U+00A0, and U+202F/2007/2009/2002/2003/3000), which breaks shell word-splitting, `grep` and column parsing while looking exactly like a space, or U+2028/U+2029, which are valid JSON and a syntax error inside a JS string literal. **Never emit an invisible or bidi character anywhere:** U+200B-U+200F, U+2060-U+2064, U+FEFF, U+00AD, U+034F, U+180E, the bidi controls U+202A-U+202E and U+2066-U+2069, and above all the Unicode tag block **U+E0000-U+E007F**, which encodes arbitrary ASCII invisibly and is the usual carrier for text a reviewer cannot see. Avoid homoglyphs (Cyrillic a/e/o/p/c/x, Greek omicron, fullwidth Latin, mathematical alphanumerics for bold): an identifier holding one compares unequal to the identifier it appears to be. Prose may use real typography and real accented names; prose may not carry characters that don't render. The one exception is a deliberate, load-bearing use, which carries a comment saying why. Scan with `rg -n '[\x{00AD}\x{034F}\x{061C}\x{180E}\x{200B}-\x{200F}\x{202A}-\x{202E}\x{2060}-\x{2064}\x{2066}-\x{2069}\x{FEFF}\x{E0000}-\x{E007F}]'`.

**Terminal output is scrollback, not a report.** Answer first — no "I'll start by", no restating the request, no narrating tool calls the transcript already shows. Don't summarise a diff the reader can see or paste back code you just wrote; one line naming what changed and where, with `file:line` because it's clickable. Length matches the question: a yes/no gets a yes/no plus the clause that makes it trustworthy, and under about six lines there are no headers, bullets or tables. Report actual output, not a paraphrase: quote the failing assertion, say what was skipped, say plainly what's verified and what isn't. No emoji and no status theatre; "246 tests, 246 passing" beats "✅ All tests passing!" and is falsifiable. Don't close with an offer of more help or unrequested next steps: ask a real question, or name the real remaining work. Commits are imperative, what and why, no launch copy. PR bodies say what changed, why, how it was verified, and what's still open.

**UI has a craft floor.** Every model trained on the same SaaS templates, so the _default_ frontend output is a recognizable handful of tells — and Tailwind + shadcn/ui puts each of them one autocomplete away. Treat the following as the category's defaults rather than as bans: the brief's own words can earn any of them, but reaching for one on a free axis means you were not deciding. Refuse **purple/blue gradients and gradient text** (emphasis comes from weight and size); **Inter or a system default as the type _choice_**; a colored **`border-left`/`border-right` above 1px** on cards, list items, callouts or alerts — the single most recognizable tell; grids of **same-size icon-tile + heading + text cards** as the page structure, and **cards nested in cards**; a **1px border under a wide soft shadow** (declare elevation once — border _or_ shadow); **gray text on colored surfaces** (tint secondary text from the surface hue or the foreground); **bounce/elastic easing**; **monospace as a costume** for "technical" rather than for code, data or measurement; and a **tracked uppercase eyebrow over every section**. Keep body measure at 65–75ch, tracking no tighter than -0.04em, and card radii at 12–16px.

**Check UI, don't just intend it.** `npx impeccable detect <path>` runs 60 deterministic anti-pattern rules with no install, no API key and no LLM — it works from any repo, so there is no excuse for asserting a UI is clean. Use the `/impeccable` skill (`audit`, `critique`, `polish`, `colorize`, `typeset`) for the judgement calls it cannot make. Source: [Impeccable](https://github.com/pbakaus/impeccable), Apache 2.0.

<!-- SELVEDGE:END -->

A WRONG ANSWER THAT READS AS A REASSURING ONE IS THE WORST SHAPE HERE (PROD-008, round 115). The last three React Native read paths were a 404, a 400 and a 404 in production, and not one of them surfaced as a failure: `/api/activities/recent` rendered "No recent activity yet", `/api/analytics/performance-metrics` rendered "--" on all three KPI cards, and `/api/equipment/:id/service-history` rendered "No service history" about machines with tickets against them. A dealer whose reps log calls all day, whose pipeline closes and whose machines get serviced saw a quiet, healthy, empty app. `/recent` is SUPA-024 exactly - the activities function reads `parts[0]` as an id, so the request became a lookup for an activity whose id is the string "recent" - and `performance-metrics` is WORSE THAN ABSENT, because the function has a `performance` branch with a different name and a completely different shape (tasks, per-user counts), so anyone grepping for the word found something and moved on. **Both new branches sit ABOVE the branch that was swallowing them**, which makes "recent" a reserved activity id: free, since these are uuids. `shared/service-history.ts` + `SERVICE_HISTORY_COLUMNS` serve BOTH the new sub-resource and the history already embedded on `GET /equipment/:id`, which selected five columns the screen does not read - two shapes for one concept inside one file is how they drift, and a test asserts the old narrow select is gone. Three rules in that mapper: an entry is dated by when the WORK happened (resolution, or the raising if still open, with `isOpen` saying which); an entry with NO date sorts LAST, because an empty string compares below every timestamp and would put an undated row at the top of a list read as chronological; and `type` is the ticket's PRIORITY with the fact written down, because `service_tickets` has no service-type column.

A RATE DIVIDES BY WHAT WAS DECIDED, NOT BY WHAT EXISTS (PROD-008, round 115). `closeRate` in `shared/mobile-dashboard.ts` divides won by won-plus-lost: a pipeline full of open work is not a pile of losses, and dividing by every deal produces a figure that FALLS whenever a rep prospects well - the opposite of what the card is read as saying. Null when nothing closed (0% claims every attempt failed), 0 when everything decided was lost, and a decided deal with no close date counted SEPARATELY rather than placed in the window, since placing it moves the rate whenever an old deal lacks a date and dropping it silently understates activity. `avgTicketTime` counts a ticket by when it was RESOLVED however long it was open - a March ticket closed in April is an April resolution with a six-week turnaround, and counting by creation hides every long one until it closes and then attributes it to a month nobody is looking at - and a resolution EARLIER than its own creation yields no interval, because that is clock skew or a backfill and a negative duration drags a mean toward a number no ticket took. `monthToDateRevenue` was EXTRACTED out of `summariseMobileDashboard` rather than copied, with a test asserting the home screen and the reports screen quote the same figure for the same month. What cannot be scoped says so: deals go through `applyUserScope` on `owner_id`/`created_by_id` and the activity feed on `created_by`, while revenue and turnaround are tenant-wide and NAMED in `unbacked` - `invoices.sales_rep` is free text and a ticket's technician is not the person reading a sales KPI, so narrowing either produces a number true of nothing.

`check:calendar-date-bounds` EARNED ITS KEEP ON A BOUND THAT WAS ALREADY CORRECT (round 115). It flagged `.gte('paid_date', previousUtcMonthStartIso(now))`, which returned midnight and was right; the guard cannot see that, and saying so was still worth it, because the fix it forced exposed a SECOND bound that was genuinely wrong. `startDate` carries the current time of day, so `.gte('actual_close_date', startDate.toISOString())` moved the edge of the window on every request - two reps opening the screen an hour apart get different denominators - and `actual_close_date` is a calendar date stored at midnight, which an afternoon bound excludes outright. One `windowStart = startOfUtcDay(startDate)` feeds both PostgREST bounds AND both summarisers, so a row cannot be fetched and then dropped, or expected and never fetched. **A guard reporting correct code is not automatically a false positive worth suppressing** - here it was a hand check nobody could repeat, and making the idiom legible found the real one next door.

TWO MUTANTS SURVIVED AND ONLY ONE WAS A TEST WEAKNESS (round 115). Removing the `at < prevStart` floor from the revenue loop survived because no fixture held an invoice older than the previous month - a real gap, closed with a 2025 invoice that must reach neither total. The other was a WEAK MUTANT rather than a weak test: dropping the `continue` after counting an undated decided deal left `new Date(null)` at epoch 0, outside every window, so the behaviour was identical. The sharper version - placing an undated deal at `now` - died once the fixture put two undated WINS beside one dated LOSS, where the wrong reading gives 67% and the right one gives 0%. Same lesson as rounds 90 and 97 in a new costume: check what the mutant actually changed, and make the fixture contain the case that separates the implementations.

THE PAGE WAS CALLING THE ONE REGISTRATION PATH THAT PROVISIONS NOTHING (LAUNCH-008, round 116). `Signup.tsx` called `supabase.auth.signUp` through `useSupabaseAuth`, which creates a GoTrue account and nothing else: the fifteen-field metadata object the page assembles lands in `user_metadata`, which `_shared/resolve-tenant.ts` ignores ON PURPOSE because the session holder can rewrite it. So every self-service registration produced an account with **no tenant row, no `users` row, no role and no `app_metadata.tenantId`**, and every edge function answered "No tenant ID found" for it - the product was unusable for anyone who came through the front door, and the story's own criteria read "CODE EXISTS (auth-routes.ts POST /signup + Signup.tsx page)" about two things that were never connected. **THREE IMPLEMENTATIONS, AND THE LIVE ONE WAS THE EMPTY ONE**: `supabase/functions/signup/` creates the tenant, resolves the seeded COMPANY_ADMIN role, writes the `users` row and sets the claims - the only one that does - and had NO CALLER in any of the seven client trees; `POST /api/auth/signup` on Express is a third and a different auth model entirely (it bcrypts a password into `users` and creates no GoTrue user, so the account cannot sign in through the client SDK).
